\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}

Here, the upper-left piece is commutative by
1.3
, the lower-left and upper-right pieces are commutative by naturality, and the lower-right piece is commutative by
2.4
. This proves the claim.

Conversely, start from an external tensor structure
(\boxtimes,m)
on
\mathbb{H}
, and let
(\boxtimes^{\prime},m^{\prime})
denote the external tensor structure obtained from it by applying first
2.11
and then
2.10
: concretely, for every
S_{1},S_{2}\in\mathscr{S}
, it is defined by the formula

A_{1}\boxtimes^{\prime}A_{2}:=\Delta_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}^{*}(pr_{1}^{*}A_{1}\boxtimes pr_{2}^{*}A_{2}).

We want to construct a canonical equivalence
e:(\boxtimes^{\prime},m^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\sim}(\boxtimes,m)
.
To this end, for every
S_{1},S_{2}\in\mathscr{S}
we define an isomorphism of functors
\mathbb{H}(S_{1})\times\mathbb{H}(S_{2})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S_{1}\times S_{2})

e=e_{S_{1},S_{2}}:A_{1}\boxtimes^{\prime}A_{2}\xrightarrow{\sim}A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2}

by taking the composite

A_{1}\boxtimes^{\prime}A_{2}:=\Delta_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}^{*}(pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1}\boxtimes pr^{12,*}_{2}A_{2})\xrightarrow{m}\Delta_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}^{*}(pr^{12}_{1}\times pr^{12}_{2})^{*}(A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2})=A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2}.

We claim that, as
S_{1},S_{2}
vary in
\mathscr{S}
, these isomorphisms satisfy condition (eq-ETS): given two morphisms
f_{i}:T_{i}\rightarrow S_{i}
in
\mathscr{S}
,
i=1,2
, we have to show that the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S_{1})\times\mathbb{H}(S_{2})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(T_{1}\times T_{2})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}^{*}A_{1}\boxtimes^{\prime}f_{2}^{*}A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}(A_{1}\boxtimes^{\prime}A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}^{*}A_{1}\boxtimes f_{2}^{*}A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}(A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}

is commutative. Unwinding the definitions, we obtain the outer part of the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{T_{1}\times T_{2}}^{*}(pr^{12,*}_{1}f_{1}^{*}A_{1}\boxtimes pr^{12}_{2}f_{2}^{*}A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{T_{1}\times T_{2}}^{*}(pr^{12}_{1}\times pr^{12}_{2})^{*}(f_{1}^{*}A_{1}\boxtimes f_{2}^{*}A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}^{*}A_{1}\boxtimes f_{2}^{*}A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{T_{1}\times T_{2}}^{*}(f_{12}^{*}pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1}\boxtimes f_{12}^{*}pr^{12,*}_{2}A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{T_{1}\times T_{2}}^{*}(f_{12}\times f_{12})^{*}(pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1}\boxtimes pr^{12,*}_{2}A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}(A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{12}^{*}\Delta_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}^{*}(pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1}\boxtimes pr^{12,*}_{2}A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{12}^{*}\Delta_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}^{*}(pr^{12}_{1}\times pr^{12}_{2})^{*}(A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}

that we decompose as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{T_{1}\times T_{2}}^{*}(pr^{12}_{1}\times pr^{12}_{2})^{*}f_{12}^{*}(A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{T_{1}\times T_{2}}^{*}((f_{1}\times f_{2})\times(f_{1}\times f_{2}))^{*}(pr^{12}_{1}\times pr^{12}_{2})^{*}(A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}

Here, the upper-left piece is commutative by
2.8
, the lower-left and upper-right pieces are commutative by naturality, and the lower-left piece is commutative by
1.3
. This proves the claim.
∎

The constructions described in the second part of this section will serve as a model for all the main constructions of this paper. In the course of the next three sections we study associativity, commutativity, and unit constraints in the setting of internal and external tensor structures. In each case, we follow the same route as above, namely: firstly, we introduce the relevant constraint both in the internal and in the external setting, and we refine the corresponding notion of equivalence accordingly; secondly, we explain how to turn an internal constraint into an external one and conversely along the constructions of
2.10
and
2.11
; thirdly, we refine the bijection of
2.12
taking the relevant constraint into account. We do not discuss how to extend the results of
2.4
and
2.8
so as to include the additional constraints, since we do not need such an extension for our applications; however, we often make use of these two auxiliary results in order to simplify our computations.

3.
Associativity constraints

The goal of this section is to introduce associativity constraint in the setting of internal and of external tensor structures and to compare them.

The notion of associativity constraint for single monoidal categories comes from
[
Mac63
, § 3]
; in the case of fibered categories, one needs to require inverse image functors to respect associativity constraints as in
[
Ayo07a
, Defn. 2.1.85(1)]
. The latter definition can be formulated in our language of internal tensor structures as follows:

Definition 3.1
.

Let
(\otimes,m)
be an internal tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}
.

(1)

An
internal associativity constraint

a
on
(\otimes,m)
is the datum of

•

for every
S\in\mathscr{S}
, a natural isomorphism between functors
\mathbb{H}(S)\times\mathbb{H}(S)\times\mathbb{H}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S)

a=a_{S}:(A\otimes B)\otimes C\xrightarrow{\sim}A\otimes(B\otimes C)

satisfying the following conditions:

(
a
ITS-1)

For every
S\in\mathscr{S}
, the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S)\times\mathbb{H}(S)\times\mathbb{H}(S)\times\mathbb{H}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((A\otimes B)\otimes C)\otimes D}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A\otimes B)\otimes(C\otimes D)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A\otimes(B\otimes C))\otimes D}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes((B\otimes C)\otimes D)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes(B\otimes(C\otimes D))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}

is commutative.

(
a
ITS-2)

For every morphism
f:T\rightarrow S
in
\mathscr{S}
, the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S)\times\mathbb{H}(S)\times\mathbb{H}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(T)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f^{*}A\otimes f^{*}B)\otimes f^{*}C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}(A\otimes B)\otimes f^{*}C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}((A\otimes B)\otimes C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}A\otimes(f^{*}B\otimes f^{*}C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}A\otimes f^{*}(B\otimes C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}(A\otimes(B\otimes C))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}

is commutative.

We say that
(\otimes,m;a)
is an
associative internal tensor structure
on
\mathbb{H}
.

(2)

Let
(\otimes,m;a)
and
(\otimes^{\prime},m^{\prime};a^{\prime})
be two associative internal tensor structures on
\mathbb{H}
. An
equivalence of associative internal tensor structures

e:(\otimes,m;a)\xrightarrow{\sim}(\otimes^{\prime},m;a^{\prime})
is an equivalence of internal tensor structures
e:(\otimes,m)\xrightarrow{\sim}(\otimes^{\prime},m^{\prime})
satisfying the following additional condition:

(eq-
a
ITS)

For every
S\in\mathscr{S}
, the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S)\times\mathbb{H}(S)\times\mathbb{H}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A\otimes B)\otimes C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A\otimes^{\prime}B)\otimes C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A\otimes^{\prime}B)\otimes^{\prime}C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes(B\otimes C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes(B\otimes^{\prime}C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes^{\prime}(B\otimes^{\prime}C)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}

is commutative.

In order to get a similar notion in the setting of external tensor structures, we just need to transliterate the previous conditions in the obvious way, as follows:

Definition 3.2
.

Let
(\boxtimes,m)
be an external tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}
.

(1)

An
external associativity constraint

a
on
(\boxtimes,m)
is the datum of

•

for every
S_{1},S_{2},S_{3}\in\mathscr{S}
, a natural isomorphism between functors
\mathbb{H}(S_{1})\times\mathbb{H}(S_{2})\times\mathbb{H}(S_{3})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S_{1}\times S_{2}\times S_{3})

a=a_{S_{1},S_{2},S_{3}}:(A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2})\boxtimes A_{3}\xrightarrow{\sim}A_{1}\boxtimes(A_{2}\boxtimes A_{3})

satisfying the following conditions:

(

ETS-1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2})\boxtimes A_{3})\boxtimes A_{4}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2})\boxtimes(A_{3}\boxtimes A_{4})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A_{1}\boxtimes(A_{2}\boxtimes A_{3}))\boxtimes A_{4}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}\boxtimes((A_{2}\boxtimes A_{3})\boxtimes A_{4})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}\boxtimes(A_{2}\boxtimes(A_{3}\boxtimes A_{4}))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}

is commutative.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1}^{*}A_{1}\boxtimes f_{2}^{*}A_{2})\boxtimes f_{3}^{*}A_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}(A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2})\boxtimes f_{3}^{*}A_{3}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1}\times f_{2}\times f_{3})^{*}(A_{1}\boxtimes(A_{2}\boxtimes A_{3}))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}

is commutative.

We say that

is an
associative external tensor structure
on
\mathbb{H}
.

Let

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}

As promised, we now describe how associativity constraints can be translated between the internal and the external setting along the constructions of

and

. This is the content of the following two results:

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}

where, for notational convenience, we have set

,

. The five unnamed natural isomorphisms are obtained by combining connection isomorphisms and internal monoidality isomorphisms along suitable paths; by

\scriptstyle\sim

Lemma 3.4
.

Let
(\boxtimes,m)
be an external tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}
, and let
(\otimes,m^{i})
denote the internal tensor structure defined from it via
2.11
. Suppose that we are given an external associativity constraint
a
on
(\boxtimes,m)
. Then, associating

•

to every
S\in\mathscr{S}
, the natural isomorphism of functors
\mathbb{H}(S)\times\mathbb{H}(S)\times\mathbb{H}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S)

a^{i}=a^{i}_{S}:(A\otimes B)\otimes C\xrightarrow{\sim}A\otimes(B\otimes C)

defined by taking the composite

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A\otimes B)\otimes C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes(B\otimes C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}(\Delta_{S}^{*}(A\boxtimes B)\boxtimes C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}(A\boxtimes\Delta_{S}^{*}(B\boxtimes C))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}(\Delta_{S}\times\textup{id}_{S})^{*}((A\boxtimes B)\boxtimes C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}(\textup{id}_{S}\times\Delta_{S})^{*}(A\boxtimes(B\boxtimes C))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{(3),*}((A\boxtimes B)\boxtimes C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{(3),*}(A\boxtimes(B\boxtimes C))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}

defines an internal associativity constraint
a^{i}
on
(\otimes,m^{i})
.

Proof.

We have to check that the definition satisfies conditions (
a
ITS-1) and (
a
ITS-2).

We start from condition (
a
ITS-1): given
S\in\mathscr{S}
, we have to show that the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S)\times\mathbb{H}(S)\times\mathbb{H}(S)\times\mathbb{H}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((A\otimes B)\otimes C)\otimes D}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A\otimes B)\otimes(C\otimes D)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A\otimes(B\otimes C))\otimes D}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes((B\otimes C)\otimes D)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes(B\otimes(C\otimes D))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{i}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{i}}

is commutative. To this ends, we decompose it as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{(4)*}(A\boxtimes((B\boxtimes C)\boxtimes D))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{(4)*}(A\boxtimes(B\boxtimes(C\boxtimes D)))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{i}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{i}}

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

Here, the five unnamed natural isomorphisms are obtained by combining connection isomorphisms and external monoidality isomorphisms along suitable paths; by

, we know that the definitions do not depend on the chosen paths. Since the central rectangle is commutative by axiom (
a
ETS-1), it suffices to show that the five lateral pieces are commutative as well. This can be achieved by the same trick mentioned in the proof of

We now treat condition (
a
ITS-2): given a morphism

in
\mathscr{S}
, we have to show that the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S)\times\mathbb{H}(S)\times\mathbb{H}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(T)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f^{*}A\otimes f^{*}B)\otimes f^{*}C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}(A\otimes B)\otimes f^{*}C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}((A\otimes B)\otimes C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}A\otimes(f^{*}B\otimes f^{*}C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}A\otimes f^{*}(B\otimes C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}(A\otimes(B\otimes C))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m^{i}}

is commutative. To this end, we decompose it as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}A\boxtimes(f^{*}B\boxtimes f^{*}C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}A\boxtimes(f\times f)^{*}(B\boxtimes C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f\times f\times f)^{*}(A\boxtimes(B\boxtimes C))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m^{i}}

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m^{i}}

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

Again, the six unnamed natural isomorphisms are obtained by combining connection isomorphisms and external monoidality isomorphisms along suitable paths, the resulting definitions being independent of the chosen paths by
2.8
. Since the central rectangle is commutative by axiom (
a
ETS-2), it suffices to show that the five lateral pieces are commutative as well. Again, this can be achieved by the same trick mentioned in the proof of
3.3
; we omit the details.
∎

Finally, we prove that the correspondence of
2.12
respects internal and external associativity constraints in a precise way:

Proposition 3.5
.

The constructions of
3.3
and
3.4
refine the canonical bijection of
2.12
to a bijection between equivalence classes of associative internal and external tensor structures on
\mathbb{H}

4.
Commutativity constraints

The goal of this section is to introduce commutativity constraints in the setting of internal and external tensor structures and to compare them.

The notion of commutativity constraint for single monoidal categories comes from
[
Mac63
, § 4]
; in the case of fibered categories, one needs to require inverse image functors to respect commutativity constraints as in
[
Ayo07a
, Defn. 2.1.86]
. The latter definition can be formulated in the language of internal tensor structures as follows:

Definition 4.1
.

Let
(\otimes,m)
be an internal tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}
.

(1)

An
internal commutativity constraint

c
on
(\otimes,m)
is the datum of

•

for every
S\in\mathscr{S}
, a natural isomorphism between functors
\mathbb{H}(S)\times\mathbb{H}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S)

c=c_{S}:A\otimes B\xrightarrow{\sim}B\otimes A

satisfying the following condition

(
c
ITS-1)

For every
S\in\mathscr{S}
, the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S)\times\mathbb{H}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\otimes A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
id
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}

is commutative.

(
c
ITS-2)

For every morphism
f:T\rightarrow S
in
\mathscr{S}
, the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S)\times\mathbb{H}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(T)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}A\otimes f^{*}B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}B\otimes f^{*}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}(A\otimes B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}(B\otimes A)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}

is commutative.

We say that
(\otimes,m;c)
is a
symmetric internal tensor structure
on
\mathbb{H}
.

(2)

Let
(\otimes,m;c)
and
(\otimes^{\prime},m^{\prime};c^{\prime})
be two symmetric internal tensor structures on
\mathbb{H}
. An
equivalence of symmetric internal tensor structures

e:(\otimes,m;c)\xrightarrow{\sim}(\otimes^{\prime},m^{\prime};c^{\prime})
is an equivalence of internal tensor structures
e:(\otimes,m)\xrightarrow{\sim}(\otimes^{\prime},m^{\prime})
satisfying the following additional condition:

(eq-
c
ITS)

For every
S\in\mathscr{S}
, the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S)\times\mathbb{H}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes^{\prime}B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\otimes A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\otimes^{\prime}A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}

is commutative.

In order to get a similar notion in the setting of external tensor structures, we need to reformulate the operation of switching factors with the help of suitable permutation isomorphisms, as follows:

Definition 4.2
.

Let
(\boxtimes,m)
be an external tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}
.

(1)

An
external commutativity constraint

c
on
(\boxtimes,m)
is the datum of

•

for every
S_{1},S_{2}\in\mathscr{S}
, a natural isomorphism between functors
\mathbb{H}(S_{1})\times\mathbb{H}(S_{2})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S_{1}\times S_{2})

c=c_{S_{1},S_{2}}:A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2}\xrightarrow{\sim}\tau^{*}(A_{2}\boxtimes A_{1}),

where
\tau=\tau_{(12)}:S_{1}\times S_{2}\rightarrow S_{2}\times S_{1}
denotes the permutation isomorphism

satisfying the following conditions:

(
c
ETS-1)

For every
S_{1},S_{2}\in\mathscr{S}
, the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S_{1})\times\mathbb{H}(S_{2})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S_{1}\times S_{2})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}(A_{2}\boxtimes A_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}\tau^{*}(A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}

is commutative.

(
c
ETS-2)

For every choice of of morphisms
f_{i}:T_{i}\rightarrow S_{i}
in
\mathscr{S}
,
i=1,2
, the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S_{1})\times\mathbb{H}(S_{2})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(T_{2}\times T_{1})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}^{*}A_{1}\boxtimes f_{2}^{*}A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}(f_{2}^{*}A_{2}\boxtimes f_{1}^{*}A_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}(A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}\tau^{*}(A_{2}\boxtimes A_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}(f_{2}\times f_{1})^{*}(A_{2}\boxtimes A_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}

is commutative.

We say that
(\boxtimes,m;c)
is a
symmetric external tensor structure
on
\mathbb{H}
.

(2)

Let
(\boxtimes^{\prime},m^{\prime};c^{\prime})
and
(\boxtimes,m;c)
be two symmetric external tensor structures on
\mathbb{H}
. An
equivalence of symmetric external tensor structures

e:(\boxtimes^{\prime},m^{\prime};c^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\sim}(\boxtimes,m;c)
is an equivalence of external tensor structures
e:(\boxtimes^{\prime},m^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\sim}(\boxtimes,m)
satisfying the following additional condition:

(eq-
c
ETS)

For every
S_{1},S_{2}\in\mathscr{S}
, the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S_{1})\times\mathbb{H}(S_{2})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S_{1}\times S_{2})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}\boxtimes^{\prime}A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}(A_{2}\boxtimes^{\prime}A_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}(A_{2}\boxtimes A_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}

is commutative.

In the same spirit as for the previous section, we now describe how commutativity constraints can be translated between the internal and the external setting along the constructions of
2.10
and
2.11
. This is the content of the following two results:

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
(\otimes,m)
be an internal tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}
, and let
(\boxtimes,m^{e})
denote the external tensor structure defined from it via
2.10
. Suppose that we are given an internal commutativity constraint
c
on
(\otimes,m)
. Then, associating

•

to every choice of
S_{1},S_{2}\in\mathscr{S}
, the natural isomorphism of functors
\mathbb{H}(S_{1})\times\mathbb{H}(S_{2})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S_{1}\times S_{2})

(6)

c^{e}=c^{e}_{S_{1},S_{2}}:A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2}\xrightarrow{\sim}\tau^{*}(A_{2}\boxtimes A_{1})

defined by taking the composite

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}(A_{2}\boxtimes A_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1}\otimes pr^{12,*}_{2}A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}(pr^{21,*}_{2}A_{2}\otimes pr^{21,*}_{1}A_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{1}A_{1}\otimes\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{2}A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{2}A_{2}\otimes\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{1}A_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}

defines an external commutativity constraint
c^{e}
on
(\boxtimes,m^{e})
.

Proof.

We have to show that the definition satisfies conditions (
c
ETS-1) and (
c
ETS-2).

We start from condition (
c
ETS-1): given
S_{1},S_{2}\in\mathscr{S}
, we have to show that the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S_{1})\times\mathbb{H}(S_{2})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S_{1}\times S_{2})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}(A_{2}\boxtimes A_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}\tau^{*}(A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{e}}

is commutative. Expanding the definitions, we obtain the outer part of the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1}\otimes pr^{12,*}_{2}A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{1}A_{1}\otimes\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{2}A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{2}A_{2}\otimes\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{1}A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}(pr^{21,*}_{2}A_{2}\otimes pr^{21,*}_{1}A_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}(\tau^{*}pr^{12,*}_{2}A_{2}\otimes\tau^{*}pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}(\tau^{*}pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1}\otimes\tau^{*}pr^{12,*}_{2}A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}\tau^{*}(pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1}\otimes pr^{12,*}_{2}A_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}

that we decompose as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{1}A_{1}\otimes\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{2}A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}(pr^{21,*}_{1}A_{1}\otimes pr^{21,*}_{2}A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
id
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}

Here, the upper central triangle is commutative by axiom (
c
ITS-1) while the remaining pieces are commutative by naturality, by axiom (
c
ITS-2) and by
2.4
. Hence the outer part of the diagram is commutative as well.

We now treat condition (
c
ETS-2): given two morphisms
f_{i}:T_{i}\rightarrow S_{i}
in
\mathscr{S}
,
i=1,2
, we have to show that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}^{*}A_{1}\boxtimes f_{2}^{*}A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}(f_{2}^{*}A_{2}\boxtimes f_{1}^{*}A_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}(A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}\tau^{*}(A_{2}\boxtimes A_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}(f_{2}\times f_{1})^{*}(A_{2}\boxtimes A_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{e}}

is commutative. Expanding the definitions, we obtain the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{1}f_{1}^{*}A_{1}\otimes\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{2}f_{2}^{*}A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{2}f_{2}^{*}A_{2}\otimes\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{1}f_{1}^{*}A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}(pr^{21,*}_{2}f_{2}^{*}A_{2}\otimes pr^{21,*}_{1}f_{1}^{*}A_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{1}f_{1}^{*}A_{1}\otimes pr^{12,*}_{2}f_{2}^{*}A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}((f_{2}\times f_{1})^{*}pr^{21,*}_{2}A_{2}\otimes(f_{2}\times f_{1})^{*}pr^{21,*}_{1}A_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1}\otimes(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}pr^{12,*}_{2}A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}(f_{2}\times f_{1})^{*}(pr^{21,*}_{2}A_{2}\otimes pr^{21,*}_{1}A_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}(pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1}\otimes pr^{12,*}_{2}A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}(\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{1}A_{1}\otimes\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{2}A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}(\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{2}A_{2}\otimes\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{1}A_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}\tau^{*}(pr^{21,*}_{2}A_{2}\otimes pr^{21,*}_{1}A_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}

that we decompose as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}pr^{21,*}_{1}A_{1}\otimes\tau^{*}(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}pr^{21,*}_{2}A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}(f_{2}\times f_{1})^{*}pr^{21,*}_{2}A_{2}\otimes\tau^{*}(f_{2}\times f_{1})^{*}pr^{21,*}_{1}A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{1}A_{1}\otimes(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{2}A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{2}A_{2}\otimes(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{1}A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}

Here, the lower-left piece is commutative by axiom (
c
ITS-2) while the other pieces are commutative by naturality, by axiom (
m
ITS) and by
1.3
. Hence the outer part of the diagram is commutative as well.
∎

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
(\boxtimes,m)
be an external tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}
, and let
(\otimes,m^{i})
denote the external tensor structure defined from it via
2.11
. Suppose that we are given an internal commutativity constraint
c
on
(\boxtimes,m)
. Then, associating

•

to every
S\in\mathscr{S}
, the natural isomorphism of functors
\mathbb{H}(S)\times\mathbb{H}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S)

c^{i}=c^{i}_{S}:A\otimes B\xrightarrow{\sim}B\otimes A

defined by taking the composite

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\otimes A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}(A\boxtimes B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}\tau^{*}(B\boxtimes A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}(B\boxtimes A)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}

defines an internal commutativity constraint
c^{i}
on
(\otimes,m^{i})
.

Proof.

We have to show that the definition satisfies conditions (
c
ITS-1) and (
c
ITS-2).

We start from condition (
c
ITS-1): given
S\in\mathscr{S}
, we have to show that the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S)\times\mathbb{H}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\otimes A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{i}}
id
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{i}}

is commutative. Expanding the definitions, we obtain the outer part of the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}(A\boxtimes B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}\tau^{*}(B\boxtimes A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}(B\boxtimes A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}\tau^{*}(A\boxtimes B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}(A\boxtimes B)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
id
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}

that we decompose as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}\tau^{*}\tau^{*}(A\boxtimes B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
id
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}

Here, the upper-right triangle is commutative by axiom (
c
ETS-1) while the remaining pieces are commutative by naturality and by
1.3
.

We now treat condition (
c
ETS-2): given a morphism
f:T\rightarrow S
in
\mathscr{S}
, we have to show that the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S)\times\mathbb{H}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(T)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}A\otimes f^{*}B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}B\otimes f^{*}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}(A\otimes B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}(B\otimes A)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{i}}

is commutative. Expanding the definitions, we obtain the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{T}^{*}(f^{*}A\boxtimes f^{*}B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{T}^{*}\tau^{*}(f^{*}B\boxtimes f^{*}A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{T}^{*}(f^{*}B\boxtimes f^{*}A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{T}^{*}(f\times f)^{*}(A\boxtimes B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{T}^{*}(f\times f)^{*}(B\boxtimes A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}\Delta_{S}^{*}(A\boxtimes B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}\Delta_{S}^{*}\tau^{*}(B\boxtimes A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}\Delta_{S}^{*}(B\boxtimes A)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}

that we decompose as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{T}^{*}\tau^{*}(f\times f)^{*}(B\boxtimes A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{T}^{*}(f\times f)^{*}\tau^{*}(B\boxtimes A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}

Here, upper-left piece is commutative by axiom (
c
ETS-2) while the other pieces are commutative by naturality and by
1.3
. This proves the claim.
∎

Proposition 4.5
.

The constructions of
4.3
and
4.4
canonically define mutually inverse bijections between equivalence classes of symmetric internal and external tensor structures on
\mathbb{H}
.

Proof.

We keep the same notation adopted in the proof of
2.12
.

Start from a symmetric internal tensor structure
(\otimes,m;c)
on
\mathbb{H}
, and let
c^{\prime}
denote the internal commutativity constraint on
(\otimes^{\prime},m)
obtained from
c
by applying first
4.3
and then
4.4
. We claim that the equivalence of internal tensor structures
e:(\otimes,m)\xrightarrow{\sim}(\otimes^{\prime},m^{\prime})
constructed in
2.12
satisfies condition (eq-
c
ITS): given
S\in\mathscr{S}
, we have to show that the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S)\times\mathbb{H}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes^{\prime}B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\otimes A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\otimes^{\prime}A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}

is commutative. Unwinding the definitions, we obtain the more explicit diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\otimes A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}pr^{12,*}_{1}A\otimes\Delta_{S}^{*}pr^{12,*}_{2}B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}pr^{21,*}_{2}B\otimes\Delta_{S}^{*}pr^{21,*}_{1}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}(pr^{12,*}_{1}A\otimes pr^{12,*}_{2}B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}(pr^{21,*}_{2}B\otimes pr^{21,*}_{1}A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}(\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{1}A\otimes\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{2}B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}(\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{2}B\otimes\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{1}A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}\tau^{*}(pr^{21,*}_{2}B\otimes pr^{21,*}_{1}A)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}

that we decompose as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}pr^{12,*}_{2}B\otimes\Delta_{S}^{*}pr^{12,*}_{1}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}(pr^{12*}_{2}B\otimes pr^{12,*}_{1}A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{2}B\otimes\Delta_{S}^{*}\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{1}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}

Here, the left central piece is commutative by axiom (
c
ITS-2) while the other pieces are commutative by axiom (
m
ITS) and by naturality. This proves the claim.

Conversely, start from a symmetric external tensor structure
(\boxtimes,m;c)
on
\mathbb{H}
, and let
c^{\prime}
denote the commutativity constraint on
(\boxtimes^{\prime},m^{\prime})
obtained from
c
by applying first
4.4
and then
4.3
. We claim that the equivalence of external tensor structures
e:(\boxtimes^{\prime},m^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\sim}(\boxtimes,m)
constructed in the proof of
2.12
satisfies condition (eq-
c
ETS): given
S_{1},S_{2}\in\mathscr{S}
, we have to show that the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S_{1})\times\mathbb{H}(S_{2})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S_{1}\times S_{2})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}\boxtimes^{\prime}A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}(A_{2}\boxtimes^{\prime}A_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}(A_{2}\boxtimes A_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}

is commutative. Unwinding the definitions, we obtain the more explicit diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}(A_{2}\boxtimes A_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}^{*}(pr^{12}_{1}\times pr^{12}_{2})^{*}(A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}\Delta_{S_{2}\times S_{1}}^{*}(pr^{21}_{2}\times pr^{21}_{1})^{*}(A_{2}\boxtimes A_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}\Delta_{S_{2}\times S_{1}}^{*}(pr^{21,*}_{2}A_{2}\boxtimes pr^{21,*}_{1}A_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}^{*}(pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1}\boxtimes pr^{12,*}_{2}A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}^{*}(\tau\times\tau)^{*}(pr^{21,*}_{2}A_{2}\boxtimes pr^{21,*}_{1}A_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}^{*}(\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{1}A_{1}\boxtimes\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{2}A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}^{*}\hat{\tau}^{*}(\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{2}A_{2}\boxtimes\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{1}A_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}^{*}(\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{2}A_{2}\boxtimes\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{1}A_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}

that we decompose as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}^{*}(pr^{12}_{1}\times pr^{12}_{2})^{*}\hat{\tau}^{*}(A_{2}\boxtimes A_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}^{*}(\tau\times\tau)^{*}(pr^{21}_{2}\times pr^{21}_{1})^{*}(A_{2}\boxtimes A_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}^{*}\hat{\tau}^{*}(pr^{12}_{2}\times pr^{12}_{1})^{*}(A_{2}\boxtimes A_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}^{*}\hat{\tau}^{*}(\tau\times\tau)^{*}(pr^{21}_{2}\times pr^{21}_{1})^{*}(A_{2}\boxtimes A_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}^{*}\hat{\tau}^{*}(pr^{12,*}_{2}A_{2}\boxtimes pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}^{*}\hat{\tau}^{*}(\tau\times\tau)^{*}(pr^{21,*}_{2}A_{2}\boxtimes pr^{21,*}_{1}A_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}

Here, the central-left piece is commutative by axiom (
c
ETS-2) while the other pieces are commutative by axiom (
m
ETS) and by naturality. This proves the claim and concludes the proof.
∎

5.
Unit constraints

The goal of this section is to introduce unit constraints in the setting of internal and external tensor structures and to compare them; in particular, we introduce unit sections following the general notions fixed in
Section

1
.

The notion of unit constraint for a single monoidal category comes from
[
Mac63
, § 5]
; in the case of fibered categories, one needs to require inverse image functors to respect unit constraints as in
[
Ayo07a
, Defn. 2.1.85(2)]
. The latter definition can be formulated in the language of internal tensor structures as follows:

Definition 5.1
.

Let
(\otimes,m)
be an internal tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}
.

(1)

An
internal unit constraint

u
on
(\otimes,m)
is the datum of

•

a section
\mathbf{1}
of the
\mathscr{S}
-fibered category
\mathbb{H}
, called the
unit section
,

•

for every
S\in\mathscr{S}
, two natural isomorphisms of functors
\mathbb{H}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S)

u_{r}=u_{r,S}:A\otimes\mathbf{1}_{S}\xrightarrow{\sim}A,\qquad\qquad u_{l}=u_{l,S}:\mathbf{1}_{S}\otimes B\xrightarrow{\sim}B

satisfying the following conditions:

(
u
ITS-0)

For every
S\in\mathscr{S}
, the diagram in
\mathbb{H}(S)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}_{S}\otimes\mathbf{1}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}_{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}

is commutative.

(
u
ITS-1)

For every morphism
f:T\rightarrow S
in
\mathscr{S}
, the two diagrams of functors
\mathbb{H}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(T)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}A\otimes f^{*}\mathbf{1}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}A\otimes\mathbf{1}_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}(A\otimes\mathbf{1}_{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}\mathbf{1}_{S}\otimes f^{*}B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}_{T}\otimes f^{*}B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}(\mathbf{1}_{S}\otimes B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}

are commutative.

We say that
(\otimes,m;u)
is a
unitary internal tensor structure
on
\mathbb{H}
.

(2)

Let
(\otimes,m;u)
and
(\otimes^{\prime},m^{\prime};u^{\prime})
be unitary internal tensor structures on
\mathbb{H}
. An
equivalence of unitary internal tensor structures

e:(\otimes,m;u)\xrightarrow{\sim}(\otimes^{\prime},m^{\prime};u^{\prime})
is the datum of

•

an equivalence of internal tensor structures
e:(\otimes,m)\xrightarrow{\sim}(\otimes^{\prime},m^{\prime})
,

•

an isomorphism of unit sections
w:\mathbf{1}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbf{1}^{\prime}

satisfying the following additional condition:

(eq-
u
ITS)

For every
S\in\mathscr{S}
, the two diagrams of functors
\mathbb{H}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes\mathbf{1}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes\mathbf{1}^{\prime}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes^{\prime}\mathbf{1}^{\prime}_{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{\prime}_{r}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}_{S}\otimes B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{\prime}_{S}\otimes B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{\prime}_{S}\otimes^{\prime}B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{\prime}_{l}}

are commutative.

In order to obtain a similar notion of the setting of external tensor structures, we need to reformulate the action of unit objects with the help of suitable projection morphisms, as follows:

Definition 5.2
.

Let
(\boxtimes,m)
be an external tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}
.

(1)

An
external unit constraint

u
on
(\boxtimes,m)
is the datum of

•

a section
\mathbf{1}
of the
\mathscr{S}
-fibered category
\mathbb{H}
, called the
unit section
,

•

for every
S_{1},S_{2}\in\mathscr{S}
, a natural isomorphism of functors
\mathbb{H}(S_{1})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S_{1}\times S_{2})

u_{r}=u_{r,S_{1},S_{2}}:A_{1}\boxtimes\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}}\xrightarrow{\sim}pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1}

as well as a natural isomorphism of functors
\mathbb{H}(S_{2})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S_{1}\times S_{2})

u_{l}=u_{l,S_{1},S_{2}}:\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}}\boxtimes A_{2}\xrightarrow{\sim}pr^{12,*}_{2}A_{2}

satisfying the following conditions:

(
u
ETS-0)

For every
S\in\mathscr{S}
, the diagram in
\mathbb{H}(S)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{1}\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}}\boxtimes\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{2}\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}

is commutative.

(
u
ETS-1)

For every choice of morphisms
f_{i}:T_{i}\rightarrow S_{i}
in
\mathscr{S}
,
i=1,2
, the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S_{1})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(T_{1}\times T_{2})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}^{*}A_{1}\boxtimes f_{2}^{*}\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}^{*}A_{1}\boxtimes\mathbf{1}_{T_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{1}f_{1}^{*}A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}(A_{1}\boxtimes\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}

and the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S_{2})\times\mathbb{H}(T_{1}\times T_{2})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}^{*}\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}}\boxtimes f_{2}^{*}A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}_{T_{1}}\boxtimes f_{2}^{*}A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{2}f_{2}^{*}A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}(\mathbf{1}_{T_{1}}\boxtimes A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}pr^{12,*}_{2}A_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}

are commutative.

We say that
(\boxtimes,m;u)
is a
unitary external tensor structure
on
\mathbb{H}
.

(2)

Let
(\boxtimes,m;u)
and
(\boxtimes^{\prime},m^{\prime};u^{\prime})
be unitary external tensor structures on
\mathbb{H}
. An
equivalence of unitary external tensor structures

e:(\boxtimes,m;u)\xrightarrow{\sim}(\boxtimes^{\prime},m^{\prime};u^{\prime})
is the datum of

•

an equivalence of internal tensor structures
e:(\otimes,m)\xrightarrow{\sim}(\otimes^{\prime},m^{\prime})
,

•

an isomorphism of unit sections
w:\mathbf{1}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbf{1}^{\prime}

satisfying the following additional condition:

(eq-
u
ETS)

For every
S_{1},S_{2}\in\mathscr{S}
, the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S_{1})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S_{1}\times S_{2})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}\boxtimes^{\prime}\mathbf{1}^{\prime}_{S_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}\boxtimes\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}\boxtimes\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{\prime}_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{\prime}_{r}}

and the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S_{2})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S_{1}\times S_{2})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}}\boxtimes^{\prime}A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{2}A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}}\boxtimes A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}}\boxtimes A_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{\prime}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}

are commutative.

In the same spirit as for the previous two sections, we now describe how unit constraints can be translated between the internal and the external setting along the constructions of
2.10
and
2.11
. This is the content of the following two results:

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
(\otimes,m)
be an internal tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}
, and let
(\boxtimes,m^{e})
denote the external tensor structure defined from it as in
2.10
. Suppose that we are given an internal unit constraint
u
(with unit section
\mathbf{1}
) on
(\otimes,m)
. Then, associating

•

to every
S_{1},S_{2}\in\mathscr{S}
, the natural isomorphism of functors
\mathbb{H}(S_{1})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S_{1}\times S_{2})

u_{r}^{e}:A_{1}\boxtimes\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}}:=pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1}\otimes pr^{12,*}_{2}\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}}\xrightarrow{\mathbf{1}^{*}}pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1}\otimes\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}\xrightarrow{u_{r}}pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1}

and the natural isomorphism of functors
\mathbb{H}(S_{2})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S_{1}\times S_{2})

u_{l}^{e}:\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}}\boxtimes A_{2}:=pr^{12,*}_{1}\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}}\otimes pr^{12,*}_{2}A_{2}\xrightarrow{\mathbf{1}^{*}}\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}\otimes pr^{12,*}_{2}A_{2}\xrightarrow{u_{l}}pr^{12,*}_{2}A_{2}

defines an external unit constraint
u^{e}
with unit section
\mathbf{1}
on
(\boxtimes,m^{e})
.

Proof.

We have to show that the definition satisfies conditions (
u
ETS-0) and (
u
ETS-1).

We start from condition (
u
ETS-0): given
S_{1},S_{2}\in\mathscr{S}
, we have to show that the diagram in
\mathbb{H}(S)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{1}\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}}\boxtimes\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{2}\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}

is commutative. Unwinding the various definitions, we obtain the more explicit diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{1}\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}}\otimes\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{1}\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}}\otimes pr^{12,*}_{2}\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}\otimes pr^{12,*}_{2}\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{1}\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{2}\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}

that we decompose as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}\otimes\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}

Here, the central piece is commutative by axiom (
u
ITS-0) while the other pieces are commutative by naturality and by axiom (
\mathscr{S}
-sect). Hence the outer part of the diagram is commutative as well.

We now treat condition (
u
EST-1): given two morphisms
f_{i}:T_{i}\rightarrow S_{i}
in
\mathscr{S}
,
i=1,2
, we have to show that the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S_{1})\times\mathbb{H}(T_{1}\times T_{2})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}^{*}A_{1}\boxtimes f_{2}^{*}\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}^{*}A_{1}\boxtimes\mathbf{1}_{T_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{1}f_{1}^{*}A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}(A_{1}\boxtimes\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}

and the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S_{2})\times\mathbb{H}(T_{1}\times T_{2})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}^{*}\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}}\boxtimes f_{2}^{*}A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}_{T_{1}}\boxtimes f_{2}^{*}A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{2}f_{2}^{*}A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}(\mathbf{1}_{T_{1}}\boxtimes A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}pr^{12,*}_{2}A_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}

are commutative. We only check the commutativity of the first diagram; the case of the second diagram is analogous. Unwinding the various definitions, we obtain the more explicit diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{1}f_{1}^{*}A_{1}\otimes pr^{12,*}_{2}f_{2}^{*}\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{1}f_{1}^{*}A_{1}\otimes pr^{12,*}_{2}\mathbf{1}_{T_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{1}f_{1}^{*}A_{1}\otimes\mathbf{1}_{T_{1}\times T_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{1}f_{1}^{*}A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{12}^{*}pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1}\otimes f_{12}^{*}pr^{12,*}_{2}\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{12}^{*}(pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1}\otimes pr^{12,*}_{2}\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{12}^{*}(pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1}\otimes\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}\times S_{2}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{12}^{*}pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}

that we decompose as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{12}^{*}pr^{12}_{1}A_{1}\otimes f_{12}^{*}\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{12}^{*}pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1}\otimes\mathbf{1}_{T_{1}\times T_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}

Here, the lower-right piece is commutative by axiom (
u
ITS-1) while the other pieces are commutative by naturality and by axiom (
\mathscr{S}
-sect). Hence the outer part of the diagram is commutative as well.
∎

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
(\boxtimes,m)
be an external tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}
, and let
(\otimes,m^{i})
denote the internal tensor structure defined from it as in
2.11
. Suppose that we are given an external unit constraint
u
(with unit section
\mathbf{1}
) on
(\boxtimes,m)
. Then, associating

•

to every
S\in\mathscr{S}
, the two natural isomorphisms of functors
\mathbb{H}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S)

u_{r}^{i}:A\otimes\mathbf{1}_{S}:=\Delta_{S}^{*}(A\boxtimes\mathbf{1}_{S})\xrightarrow{u_{r}}\Delta_{S}^{*}pr^{12,*}_{1}A=A,\quad u_{l}^{i}:\mathbf{1}_{S}\otimes A:=\Delta_{S}^{*}(\mathbf{1}_{S}\boxtimes A)\xrightarrow{u_{l}}\Delta_{S}^{*}pr^{12,*}_{2}A=A

defines an external unit constraint
u^{i}
(with unit section
\mathbf{1}
) on
(\otimes,m^{i})
.

Proof.

We have to show that the definition satisfies conditions (
u
ITS-0) and (
u
ITS-1).

We start from condition (
u
ITS-0): given
S\in\mathscr{S}
, we have to show that the diagram in
\mathbb{H}(S)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}_{S}\otimes\mathbf{1}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}_{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}

is commutative. Unwinding the various definitions, we obtain the more explicit diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}pr^{12,*}_{1}\mathbf{1}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}(\mathbf{1}_{S}\boxtimes\mathbf{1}_{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}pr^{12,*}_{2}\mathbf{1}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}_{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}

that we decompose as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}\mathbf{1}_{S\times S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}

Here, the upper piece is commutative by axiom (
u
ITS-0) while the other pieces are commutative by axiom (
\mathscr{S}
-sect). Hence the outer part of the diagram is commutative as well.

We now treat condition (
u
ITS-1): given a morphism
f:T\rightarrow S
in
\mathscr{S}
, we have to show that the two diagrams of functors
\mathbb{H}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(T)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}A\otimes f^{*}\mathbf{1}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}A\otimes\mathbf{1}_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}(A\otimes\mathbf{1}_{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}\mathbf{1}_{S}\otimes f^{*}B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}_{T}\otimes f^{*}B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}(\mathbf{1}_{S}\otimes B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}

are commutative. We only check the commutativity of the first diagram; the case of the second diagram is analogous. Unwinding the various definitions, we obtain the more explicit diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{T}^{*}(f^{*}A\boxtimes f^{*}\mathbf{1}_{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{T}^{*}(f^{*}A\boxtimes\mathbf{1}_{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{T}^{*}pr^{12,*}_{1}f^{*}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{T}^{*}(f\times f)^{*}(A\boxtimes\mathbf{1}_{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}\Delta_{S}^{*}(A\boxtimes\mathbf{1}_{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}\Delta_{S}^{*}pr^{12,*}_{1}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}

that we decompose as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{T}^{*}(f\times f)^{*}pr^{12,*}_{1}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}

Here, the upper piece is commutative by axiom (
u
EST-1) while the other pieces are commutative by naturality and by
1.3
. Hence the outer part of the diagram is commutative as well.
∎

Proposition 5.5
.

The constructions of
5.3
and
5.4
canonically define mutually inverse bijections between equivalence classes of unitary internal and external tensor structures with a given unit section
\mathbf{1}
on
\mathbb{H}
.

Proof.

We keep the same notation as in the proof of
2.12
. Throughout the proof, we fix a section
\mathbf{1}
of the
\mathscr{S}
-fibered category
\mathbb{H}
; the only isomorphism of unit section involved in the proof is the identity isomorphism
\textup{id}_{\mathbf{1}}:\mathbf{1}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbf{1}
.

Start from a unitary internal tensor structure
(\otimes,m;u)
, and let
u^{\prime}
denote the internal unit constraint on
(\otimes^{\prime},m^{\prime})
obtained from
u
by applying first
5.3
and then
5.4
. We claim that the equivalence of internal tensor structures
e:(\otimes,m)\xrightarrow{\sim}(\otimes^{\prime},m^{\prime})
constructed in the first part of the proof of
2.12
satisfies condition (eq-
u
ITS): given
S\in\mathscr{S}
, we have to show that the two diagrams of functors
\mathbb{H}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes\mathbf{1}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes^{\prime}\mathbf{1}_{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{\prime}_{r}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}_{S}\otimes A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes^{\prime}\mathbf{1}_{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{\prime}_{l}}

are commutative. We only check the commutativity of the diagram on the left; the case of the diagram on the right is completely analogous. Unwinding the various definitions, we obtain the more explicit diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes\mathbf{1}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}pr^{12,*}_{1}A\otimes\Delta_{S}^{*}pr^{12,*}_{2}\mathbf{1}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}(pr^{12,*}_{1}A\otimes pr^{12,*}_{2}\mathbf{1}_{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}(pr^{12,*}_{1}A\otimes\mathbf{1}_{S\times S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}pr^{12,*}_{1}A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}

that we decompose as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes\Delta_{S}^{*}pr^{12,*}_{2}\mathbf{1}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes\Delta_{S}^{*}\mathbf{1}_{S\times S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}pr^{12,*}_{1}A\otimes\Delta_{S}^{*}\mathbf{1}_{S\times S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes\mathbf{1}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}pr^{12,*}_{1}A\otimes\mathbf{1}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}

Here, the right-most piece is commutative by axiom (
u
ITS-1) while the other pieces are commutative by naturality and by construction. This proves the claim.

Conversely, start from a unitary external tensor structure
(\boxtimes,m;u)
on
\mathbb{H}
, and let
u^{\prime}
denote the external unit constraint on
(\boxtimes^{\prime},m^{\prime})
obtained from
u
by applying first
5.4
and then
5.3
. We claim that the equivalence of external tensor structures
e:(\boxtimes^{\prime},m^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\sim}(\boxtimes,m)
constructed in the second part of the proof of
2.12
satisfies condition (eq-
u
ETS): given
S_{1},S_{2}\in\mathscr{S}
, we have to show that the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S_{1})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S_{1}\times S_{2})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}\boxtimes^{\prime}\mathbf{1}^{\prime}_{S_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}\boxtimes\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{\prime}_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{\prime}_{r}}

and the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S_{2})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S_{1}\times S_{2})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}}\boxtimes^{\prime}A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}}\boxtimes A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{2}A_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{\prime}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}

are commutative. We only show the commutativity of the first diagram; the case of the second diagram is completely analogous. Unwinding the various definitions, we obtain the more explicit diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}^{*}(pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1}\boxtimes\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}\boxtimes S_{2}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}^{*}(pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1}\boxtimes pr^{12,*}_{2}\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}^{*}(pr^{12}_{1}\times pr^{12}_{2})^{*}(A_{1}\boxtimes\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}\boxtimes\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}^{*}pr^{1212,*}_{12}pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}

that we decompose as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}^{*}(pr^{12}_{1}\times pr^{12}_{2})^{*}pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}

Here, the upper-left triangle is commutative by axiom (
u
ETS-1) while the other pieces are commutative by naturality and by
2.8
. This proves the claim and concludes the proof.
∎

6.
Compatibility conditions

After the discussion about associativity, commutativity and unit constraints developed in the previous sections, we are ready to state our first main result:

Theorem 6.1
.

There exist canonical mutually inverse bijections between equivalence classes of (unitary, symmetric, associative) internal and external tensor structures on
\mathbb{H}
.

The bijections in the statement, of course, are those of
2.12
; the results of
3.5
,
4.5
and
5.5
refine them so as to include associativity, symmetry, and unitarity, respectively. In order to complete the picture, we need to study the mutual compatibility conditions between any two of these three types of constraints: this is the main goal of the present section.

The notion of compatibility just mentioned refers to a property rather than to an additional structure. Therefore, its formulation in the setting of monoidal fibered categories is the same as in the setting of usual monoidal categories, which can be found in
[
Mac63
, §§ 4,5]
.

6.1.
Associativity versus commutativity

Definition 6.2
.

Let
(\otimes,m)
be an internal tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}
, endowed with an internal associativity constraint
a
and an internal commutativity constraint
c
.

We say that the constraints
a
and
c
are
compatible
if they satisfy the following condition:

(
ac
ITS)

For every
S\in\mathscr{S}
, the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S)\times\mathbb{H}(S)\times\mathbb{H}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A\otimes B)\otimes C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(B\otimes A)\otimes C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\otimes(A\otimes C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes(B\otimes C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(B\otimes C)\otimes A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\otimes(C\otimes A)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}

is commutative.

In this case, we say that
(\otimes,m;a,c)
is a
symmetric associative internal tensor structure
on
\mathbb{H}
.

Definition 6.3
.

Let
(\boxtimes,m)
be an external tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}
, endowed with an external associativity constraint
a
and an external commutativity constraint
c
.

We say that the constraints
a
and
c
are
compatible
if they satisfy the following condition:

(
ac
ETS)

For every
S_{1},S_{2},S_{3}\in\mathscr{S}
, the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S_{1})\times\mathbb{H}(S_{2})\times\mathbb{H}(S_{3})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S_{1}\times S_{2}\times S_{3})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2})\boxtimes A_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{(12)}^{*}(A_{2}\boxtimes A_{1})\boxtimes A_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{(12)(3)}^{*}((A_{2}\boxtimes A_{1})\boxtimes A_{3})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{(12)(3)}^{*}(A_{2}\boxtimes(A_{1}\boxtimes A_{3}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{(12)(3)}^{*}(A_{2}\boxtimes\tau_{(13)}^{*}(A_{3}\boxtimes A_{1}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}\boxtimes(A_{2}\boxtimes A_{3})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{(132)}^{*}((A_{2}\boxtimes A_{3})\boxtimes A_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{(132)}^{*}(A_{2}\boxtimes(A_{3}\boxtimes A_{1}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{(12)(3)}^{*}\tau_{(2)(13)}^{*}(A_{2}\boxtimes(A_{3}\boxtimes A_{1}))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}

is commutative.

In this case, we say that
(\boxtimes,m;a,c)
is a
symmetric associative external tensor structure
on
\mathbb{H}
.

Lemma 6.4
.

Let
(\otimes,m)
be an internal tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}
, and let
(\boxtimes,m^{e})
denote the external tensor structure obtained from it via
2.10
.
Suppose that
(\otimes,m)
is endowed with compatible internal associativity and commutativity constraints
a
and
c
; let
a^{e}
and
c^{e}
denote the external associativity and commutativity constraints on
(\boxtimes,m)
obtained from them via
3.3
and
3.4
, respectively.

Then the external associativity and commutativity constraints
a^{e}
and
c^{e}
are compatible as well.

Proof.

Let us check that the external constraints
a^{e}
and
c^{e}
satisfy condition (
ac
ETS): given
S_{1},S_{2},S_{3}\in\mathscr{S}
, we have to show that the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S_{1})\times\mathbb{H}(S_{2})\times\mathbb{H}(S_{3})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S_{1}\times S_{2}\times S_{3})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2})\boxtimes A_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{(12)}^{*}(A_{2}\boxtimes A_{1})\boxtimes A_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{(12)(3)}^{*}((A_{2}\boxtimes A_{1})\boxtimes A_{3})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{(12)(3)}^{*}(A_{2}\boxtimes(A_{1}\boxtimes A_{3}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{(12)(3)}^{*}(A_{2}\boxtimes\tau_{(13)}^{*}(A_{3}\boxtimes A_{1}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}\boxtimes(A_{2}\boxtimes A_{3})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{(132)}^{*}((A_{2}\boxtimes A_{3})\boxtimes A_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{(132)}^{*}(A_{2}\boxtimes(A_{3}\boxtimes A_{1}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{(12)(3)}^{*}\tau_{(2)(13)}^{*}(A_{2}\boxtimes(A_{3}\boxtimes A_{1}))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{e}}

is commutative. To this end, we decompose it as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(B_{1}\otimes B_{2})\otimes B_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(B_{2}\otimes B_{1})\otimes B_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{2}\otimes(B_{1}\otimes B_{3})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{1}\otimes(B_{2}\otimes B_{3})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(B_{2}\otimes B_{3})\otimes B_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{2}\otimes(B_{3}\otimes B_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

\scriptstyle\sim

where, for notational convenience, we have set
B_{i}:=pr^{123,*}_{i}A_{i}
,
i=1,2,3
. The six unnamed natural isomorphisms are obtained by combining connection isomorphisms and internal monoidality isomorphisms along suitable paths; by
2.4
, we know that the definitions do not depend on the chosen paths. Since the central rectangle in the latter diagram is commutative by axiom (
ac
ITS), it now suffices to show that the six lateral pieces are commutative as well. This follows by the usual trick; we omit the details.
∎

Lemma 6.5
.

Let
(\boxtimes,m)
be an external tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}
, and let
(\otimes,m^{i})
denote the internal tensor structure obtained from it via
2.11
.
Suppose that
(\boxtimes,m)
is endowed with compatible external associativity and commutativity constraints
a
and
c
; let

and
c^{i}
denote the internal associativity and commutativity constraints on
(\otimes,m^{i})
obtained from them via
3.4
and
4.4
, respectively.

Then the internal associativity and commutativity constraints
a^{i}
and
c^{i}
are compatible as well.

Proof.

Let us check that the internal constraints
a^{i}
and
c^{i}
satisfy condition (
ac
ITS): for every
S\in\mathscr{S}
, we have to show that the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S)\times\mathbb{H}(S)\times\mathbb{H}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A\otimes B)\otimes C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(B\otimes A)\otimes C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\otimes(A\otimes C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes(B\otimes C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(B\otimes C)\otimes A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\otimes(C\otimes A)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{i}}

is commutative. To this end, we decompose it as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
