{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{12}^{*}(B\boxtimes A)\boxtimes C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{(12)(3)}^{*}(B\boxtimes(A\boxtimes C))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{(12)(3)}^{*}(B\boxtimes\tau_{(13)}^{*}(C\boxtimes A))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\boxtimes(B\boxtimes C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{(132)}^{*}((B\boxtimes C)\boxtimes A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{(132)}^{*}(B\boxtimes(C\boxtimes A))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{(12)(3)}^{*}\tau_{(2)(13)}^{*}(B\boxtimes(C\boxtimes A))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{i}}

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{i}}

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

The six unnamed natural isomorphisms are obtained by combining connection isomorphisms and external monoidality isomorphisms along suitable paths; by
2.8
, we know that the definitions do not depend on the chosen paths. Since the central rectangle in the latter diagram is commutative by axiom (
ac
ETS), it now suffices to show that the six lateral pieces are commutative as well. This follows by the usual trick; we omit the details.
∎

6.2.
Associativity versus unit

Definition 6.6
.

Let
(\otimes,m)
be an internal tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}
, endowed with an internal associativity constraint
a
and an internal unit constraint
u
(with unit section
\mathbf{1}
).

We say that the constraints
a
and
u
are
compatible
if they satisfy the following condition:

(
au
ITS)

For every
S\in\mathscr{S}
, the three diagrams of functors
\mathbb{H}(S)\times\mathbb{H}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbf{1}_{S}\otimes B)\otimes C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}_{S}\otimes(B\otimes C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\otimes C}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A\otimes\mathbf{1}_{S})\otimes C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes(\mathbf{1}_{S}\otimes C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes C}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A\otimes B)\otimes\mathbf{1}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes(B\otimes\mathbf{1}_{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}

are commutative.

In this case, we say that
(\otimes,m;a,u)
is a
unitary associative internal tensor structure
on
\mathbb{H}
.

Definition 6.7
.

Let
(\boxtimes,m)
be an external tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}
, endowed with an external associativity constraint
a
and an external unit constraint
u
(with unit section
\mathbf{1}
).

We say that the constraints
a
and
u
are
compatible
if they satisfy the following condition:

(
au
ETS)

For every
S_{1},S_{2},S_{3}\in\mathscr{S}
, the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S_{2})\times\mathbb{H}(S_{3})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S_{2}\times S_{3})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}}\boxtimes A_{2})\boxtimes A_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}}\boxtimes(A_{2}\boxtimes A_{3})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{2}A_{2}\boxtimes A_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{123,*}_{23}(A_{2}\boxtimes A_{3}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}

the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S_{1})\times\mathbb{H}(S_{3})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S_{1}\times S_{3})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A_{1}\boxtimes\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}})\boxtimes A_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}\boxtimes(\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}}\boxtimes A_{3})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1}\boxtimes A_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{123,*}_{13}(A_{1}\boxtimes A_{3})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}\boxtimes pr^{23,*}_{3}A_{3}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}

and the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S_{1})\times\mathbb{H}(S_{2})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S_{1}\times S_{2})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2})\boxtimes\mathbf{1}_{S_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}\boxtimes(A_{2}\boxtimes\mathbf{1}_{S_{3}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{123,*}_{23}(A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}\boxtimes pr^{12,*}_{2}A_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}

are commutative.

In this case, we say that
(\boxtimes,m;a,u)
is a
unitary associative external tensor structure
on
\mathbb{H}
.

Lemma 6.8
.

Let
(\otimes,m)
be an internal tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}
, and let
(\boxtimes,m)
denote the external tensor structure obtained from it via
2.10
. Suppose that
(\otimes,m)
is endowed with compatible internal associativity and unit constraints
a
and
u
, and let
a^{e}
and
u^{e}
denote the external associativity and unit constraints on
(\boxtimes,m)
obtained from them via
3.3
and
5.3
, respectively.

Then the external associativity and unit constraints
a^{e}
and
u^{e}
are compatible as well.

Proof.

Let us check that the constraints
a^{e}
and
u^{e}
satisfy condition (
au
ETS): for every
S_{1},S_{2},S_{3}\in\mathscr{S}
, we have to show that the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S_{2})\times\mathbb{H}(S_{3})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S_{2}\times S_{3})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}}\boxtimes A_{2})\boxtimes A_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}}\boxtimes(A_{2}\boxtimes A_{3})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{2}A_{2}\boxtimes A_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{123,*}_{23}(A_{2}\boxtimes A_{3}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{e}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{e}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m^{e}}

the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S_{1})\times\mathbb{H}(S_{3})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S_{1}\times S_{3})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A_{1}\boxtimes\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}})\boxtimes A_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}\boxtimes(\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}}\boxtimes A_{3})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1}\boxtimes A_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{123,*}_{13}(A_{1}\boxtimes A_{3})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}\boxtimes pr^{23,*}_{3}A_{3}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{e}_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{e}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m^{e}}

and the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S_{1})\times\mathbb{H}(S_{2})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S_{1}\times S_{2})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2})\boxtimes\mathbf{1}_{S_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}\boxtimes(A_{2}\boxtimes\mathbf{1}_{S_{3}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{123,*}_{23}(A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}\boxtimes pr^{12,*}_{2}A_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{e}_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{e}_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m^{e}}

are commutative. We only show the commutativity of the first diagram; the cases of the second and third diagrams are completely analogous.
Unwinding the various definitions, we obtain the more explicit diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(pr^{123,*}_{1}\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}}\otimes pr^{123,*}_{2}A_{2})\otimes pr^{123,*}_{3}A_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{123,*}_{1}\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}}\otimes(pr^{123,*}_{2}A_{2}\otimes pr^{123,*}_{3}A_{3})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(pr^{123,*}_{12}pr^{12,*}_{1}\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}}\otimes pr^{123,*}_{23}pr^{23,*}_{2}A_{2})\otimes pr^{123,*}_{3}A_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{123,*}_{1}\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}}\otimes(pr^{123,*}_{23}pr^{23,*}_{2}A_{2}\otimes pr^{123,*}_{23}pr^{23,*}_{3}A_{3})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{123,*}_{12}(pr^{12,*}_{1}\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}}\otimes pr^{23,*}_{2}A_{2})\otimes pr^{123,*}_{3}A_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{123,*}_{1}\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}}\otimes pr^{123,*}_{23}(pr^{23,*}_{2}A_{2}\otimes pr^{23,*}_{3}A_{3})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{123,*}_{12}(\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}\otimes pr^{23,*}_{2}A_{2})\otimes pr^{123,*}_{3}A_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}\times S_{2}\times S_{3}}\otimes pr^{123,*}_{23}(pr^{23,*}_{2}A_{2}\otimes pr^{23,*}_{3}A_{3})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{123,*}_{12}pr^{23,*}_{2}A_{2}\otimes pr^{123,*}_{3}A_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{123,*}_{23}(pr^{23,*}_{2}A_{2}\otimes pr^{23,*}_{3}A_{3})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{123,*}_{2}A_{2}\otimes pr^{123,*}_{3}A_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{123,*}_{23}pr^{23,*}_{2}A_{2}\otimes pr^{123,*}_{23}pr^{23,*}_{3}A_{3}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}

that we decompose as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(pr^{123,*}_{12}\mathbf{1}_{S_{12}}\otimes pr^{123,*}_{23}pr^{23,*}_{2}A_{2})\otimes pr^{123,*}_{3}A_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbf{1}_{S_{123}}\otimes pr^{123,*}_{2}A_{2})\otimes pr^{123,*}_{3}A_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbf{1}_{S_{123}}\otimes pr^{123,*}_{23}pr^{23,*}_{2}A_{2})\otimes pr^{123,*}_{3}A_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}_{S_{123}}\otimes(pr^{123,*}_{2}A_{2}\otimes pr^{123,*}_{3}A_{3})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}_{S_{123}}\otimes(pr^{123,*}_{23}pr^{23,*}_{2}A_{2}\otimes pr^{123,*}_{23}pr^{23,*}_{3}A_{3})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}

Here, the central triangle is commutative by axiom (
au
ITS) while the remaining pieces are commutative by naturality and by axioms (
\mathscr{S}
-sect) and (
u
ITS-1). This proves the claim.
∎

Lemma 6.9
.

Let
(\boxtimes,m)
be an external tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}
, and let
(\otimes,m)
denote the internal tensor structure obtained from it via
2.11
. Suppose that
(\boxtimes,m)
is endowed with compatible external associativity and unit constraints
a
and
u
, and let
a^{i}
and
u^{i}
denote the internal associativity and unit constraints on
(\otimes,m)
obtained from them via
3.4
and
5.4
, respectively.

Then the internal associativity and unit constraints
a^{i}
and
u^{i}
are compatible as well.

Proof.

Let us check that the constraints
a^{i}
and
u^{i}
in the statement satisfy condition (
au
ITS): for every
S\in\mathscr{S}
, we have to show that the three diagrams of functors
\mathbb{H}(S)\times\mathbb{H}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbf{1}_{S}\otimes B)\otimes C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}_{S}\otimes(B\otimes C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\otimes C}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{i}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{i}_{l}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A\otimes\mathbf{1}_{S})\otimes C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes(\mathbf{1}_{S}\otimes C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes C}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{i}_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{i}_{l}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A\otimes B)\otimes\mathbf{1}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes(B\otimes\mathbf{1}_{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{i}_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{i}_{r}}

are commutative. We only show the commutativity of the first diagram; the cases of the second and third diagram are completely analogous.
Unwinding the various definitions, we obtain the more explicit diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{(3),*}((\mathbf{1}_{S}\boxtimes B)\boxtimes C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{(3),*}(\mathbf{1}_{S}\boxtimes(B\boxtimes C))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}(\Delta_{S}\times\textup{id}_{S})^{*}((\mathbf{1}_{S}\boxtimes B)\boxtimes C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}(\textup{id}_{S}\times\Delta_{S})^{*}(\mathbf{1}_{S}\boxtimes(B\boxtimes C))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}(\Delta_{S}^{*}(\mathbf{1}_{S}\boxtimes B)\boxtimes C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}(\mathbf{1}_{S}\boxtimes\Delta^{*}(B\boxtimes C))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}(\Delta^{*}pr^{12,*}_{2}B\boxtimes C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}(B\boxtimes C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}pr^{12,*}_{2}\Delta_{S}^{*}(B\boxtimes C)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{m}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}

that we decompose as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{(3),*}(pr^{12,*}_{2}B\boxtimes C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{(3),*}pr^{123,*}_{23}(B\boxtimes C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}(\Delta_{S}\times\textup{id}_{S})^{*}(pr^{12,*}_{2}B\boxtimes C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}(\Delta_{S}\times\textup{id}_{S})^{*}pr^{123,*}_{23}(B\boxtimes C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}(\textup{id}_{S}\times\Delta_{S})^{*}pr^{123,*}_{23}(B\boxtimes C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}

Here, the upper piece is commutative by axiom (
au
ETS) while the remaining pieces are commutative by naturality, by
2.8
and by axiom (
u
ETS-1). This proves the claim.
∎

6.3.
Commutativity versus unit

Definition 6.10
.

Let
(\otimes,m)
be an internal tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}
, endowed with an internal commutativity constraint
c
and an internal unit constraint
u
(with unit section
\mathbf{1}
).

We say that the constraints
c
and
u
are
compatible
if they satisfy the following condition:

(
cu
ITS)

For every
S\in\mathscr{S}
, the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}_{S}\otimes B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\otimes\mathbf{1}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}

is commutative.

In this case, we say that
(\otimes,m;c,u)
is a
unitary symmetric internal tensor structure
on
\mathbb{H}
.

Definition 6.11
.

Let
(\boxtimes,m)
be an external tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}
, endowed with an external commutativity constraint
c
and an external unit constraint
u
(with unit section
\mathbf{1}
).

We say that the constraints
c
and
u
are
compatible
if they satisfy the following condition:

(
cu
ETS)

For every
S_{1},S_{2}\in\mathscr{S}
, the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S_{2})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S_{1}\times S_{2})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}}\otimes B_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}(B_{2}\otimes\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{2}B_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{2}B_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}

is commutative.

In this case, we say that
(\boxtimes,m;c,u)
is a
unitary symmetric external tensor structure
on
\mathbb{H}
.

Lemma 6.12
.

Let
(\otimes,m)
be an internal tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}
, and let
(\boxtimes,m)
denote the external tensor structure obtained from it via
2.10
. Suppose that
(\otimes,m)
is endowed with compatible internal commutativity and unit constraints
c
and
u
, and let
c^{e}
and
u^{e}
denote the external commutativity and unit constraints on

obtained from them via
4.3
and
5.3
, respectively.

Then the external commutativity and unit constraints
c^{e}
and
u^{e}
are compatible as well.

Proof.

Let us check that the constraints
c^{e}
and
u^{e}
in the statement satisfy condition (

S_{1},S_{2}\in\mathscr{S}
, we have to show that the diagram of functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}}\otimes B_{2}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{2}B_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{2}B_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{e}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{e}_{r}}

is commutative.
Unwinding the various definitions, we obtain the more explicit diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{1}\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}}\otimes\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{2}A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{2}A_{2}\otimes\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{1}\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{1}\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}}\otimes pr^{12,*}_{2}A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}(pr^{21,*}_{2}A_{2}\otimes pr^{21,*}_{1}\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}\otimes pr^{12,*}_{2}A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}(pr^{21,*}_{2}A_{2}\otimes\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}\times S_{1}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{2}A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{2}A_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}

that we decompose as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}\times S_{1}}\otimes pr^{12,*}_{2}A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}\times S_{1}}\otimes\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{2}A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{2}A_{2}\otimes\tau^{*}\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}\times S_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}\otimes\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{2}A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{*}(\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}\times S_{1}}\otimes pr^{21,*}_{2}A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}

Here, the lower-right piece is commutative by axiom (
cu
ITS) while the remaining pieces are commutative by naturality and by axiom (
\mathscr{S}
-sect). This concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma 6.13
.

Let
(\boxtimes,m)
be an external tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}
, and let
(\otimes,m)
denote the internal tensor structure obtained from it via
2.11
. Suppose that
(\boxtimes,m)
is endowed with compatible external commutativity and unit constraints
c
and
u
, and let
c^{i}
and
u^{i}
denote the internal commutativity and unit constraints on
(\otimes,m)
obtained from them via
4.4
and
5.4
, respectively.

Then the internal commutativity and unit constraints
c^{i}
and
u^{i}
are compatible as well.

Proof.

Let us check that the constraints
c^{i}
and
u^{i}
in the statement satisfy condition (
cu
ITS): for every
S\in\mathscr{S}
, we have to show that the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}_{S}\otimes B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\otimes\mathbf{1}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{i}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{i}_{r}}

is commutative.
Unwinding the various definitions, we obtain the more explicit diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}(\mathbf{1}_{S}\boxtimes B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}\tau^{*}(B\boxtimes\mathbf{1}_{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}(B\boxtimes\mathbf{1}_{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}pr^{12,*}_{2}B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}pr^{21,*}_{1}B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}

that we decompose as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}\tau^{*}pr^{21,*}_{2}B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}

Here, the upper-left piece is commutative by axiom (
cu
ETS) while the remaining pieces are commutative by naturality. This concludes the proof.
∎

7.
Projection formulae

In many concrete geometric situations, one is interested in monoidal fibered categories satisfying the so-called projection formulae. The example that we have in mind is that of stable homotopic
2
-functors studied in
[
Ayo07a
]
and
[
Ayo07b
]
: in this case, the relevant definition is
[
Ayo07a
, Defn. 2.3.1]
.

In this short section, we study the validity of projection formulae in the abstract setting of internal and external tensor structures. To this end, we need to introduce more structure on the base category
\mathscr{S}
.

Hypothesis 7.1
.

We assume that the category
\mathscr{S}
is endowed with a distinguished subcategory
\mathscr{S}^{sm}
with the following property: For every Cartesian square in
\mathscr{S}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

with
p
in
\mathscr{S}^{sm}
, the morphism
p^{\prime}
belongs to
\mathscr{S}^{sm}
as well.
We call
smooth morphisms
the morphisms in
\mathscr{S}^{sm}
.

Example 7.2
.

The motivating examples are when
\mathscr{S}=\mathsf{Var}_{k}
is the category of (quasi-projective) algebraic varieties over a field
k
and
\mathscr{S}^{sm}
denotes its subcategory of smooth morphisms. The same discussion also applies when
\mathscr{S}=\mathsf{Sm}_{k}
is the category of smooth (quasi-projective)
k
-varieties and
\mathscr{S}^{sm}
denotes again its subcategory of smooth morphisms.

Definition 7.3
.

We say that the
\mathscr{S}
-fibered category
\mathbb{H}
is
geometric
if it satisfies the following two conditions:

(
\mathscr{S}^{sm}
-0)

For every smooth morphism
p:P\rightarrow S
in
\mathscr{S}
, the functor
p^{*}:\mathbb{H}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(P)
admits a left-adjoint
p_{\#}:\mathbb{H}(P)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S)
.

(
\mathscr{S}^{sm}
-1)

For every Cartesian square in
\mathscr{S}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

where
p
(and hence
p^{\prime}
) is a smooth morphism, the natural transformation of functors
\mathbb{H}(P)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S^{\prime})

{p^{\prime}}_{\#}{g^{\prime}}^{*}A\xrightarrow{\eta}{p^{\prime}}_{\#}{g^{\prime}}^{*}p^{*}p_{\#}A={p^{\prime}}_{\#}{p^{\prime}}^{*}g^{*}p_{\#}A\xrightarrow{\epsilon}g^{*}p_{\#}A

is invertible.

Throughout this section, we fix a geometric
\mathscr{S}
-fibered category
\mathbb{H}
with respect to
\mathscr{S}^{sm}
.

Following
[
Ayo07a
, Defn. 2.3.1]
, we introduce the projection formulae in the setting of internal tensor structures:

Definition 7.4
.

We say that an internal tensor structure
(\otimes,m)
on
\mathbb{H}
is
geometric
if the following condition holds:

(ITS-pf)

For every smooth morphism
p:P\rightarrow S
, the natural transformation of functors
\mathbb{H}(P)\times\mathbb{H}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S)

(7)

p_{\#}(A\otimes p^{*}B)\xrightarrow{\eta}p_{\#}(p^{*}p_{\#}A\otimes p^{*}B)\xrightarrow{m}p_{\#}p^{*}(p_{\#}A\otimes B)\xrightarrow{\epsilon}p_{\#}A\otimes B

and the natural transformation of functors
\mathbb{H}(S)\times\mathbb{H}(P)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S)

p_{\#}(p^{*}A\otimes B)\xrightarrow{\eta}p_{\#}(p^{*}A\otimes p^{*}p_{\#}B)\xrightarrow{m}p_{\#}p^{*}(A\otimes p_{\#}B)\xrightarrow{\epsilon}A\otimes p_{\#}B

are invertible.

With some minor modifications, we obtain the analogous definition in the setting of external tensor structures:

Definition 7.5
.

We say that an external tensor structure
(\boxtimes,m)
on
\mathbb{H}
is
geometric
if the the following condition holds:

(ETS-pf)

For every choice of smooth morphisms
p_{i}:P_{i}\rightarrow S_{i}
,
i=1,2
, the natural transformation of functors
\mathbb{H}(P_{1})\times\mathbb{H}(S_{2})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S_{1}\times S_{2})

(8)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(p_{1}\times\textup{id}_{S_{2}})_{\#}(A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1,\#}A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(p_{1}\times\textup{id}_{S_{2}})_{\#}(p_{1}^{*}p_{1,\#}A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(p_{1}\times\textup{id}_{S_{2}})_{\#}(p_{1}\times\textup{id}_{S_{2}})^{*}(p_{1,\#}A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon}

and the natural transformation of functors
\mathbb{H}(S_{1})\times\mathbb{H}(P_{2})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S_{1}\times S_{2})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\textup{id}_{S_{1}}\times p_{2})_{\#}(A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}\boxtimes p_{2,\#}A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\textup{id}_{S_{1}}\times p_{2})_{\#}(A_{1}\boxtimes p_{2}^{*}p_{2,\#}A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\textup{id}_{S_{1}}\times p_{2})_{\#}(\textup{id}_{S_{1}}\times p_{2})^{*}(A_{1}\boxtimes p_{2,\#}A_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon}

are invertible.

Remark 7.6
.

Both in the setting of internal and external tensor structures, the property of being geometric is clearly stable under equivalence.

Here is the only result of the present section:

Proposition 7.7
.

Let
\mathbb{H}
be a geometric
\mathscr{S}
-fibered category. Let
(\otimes,m^{i})
be an internal tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}
, and let
(\boxtimes,m^{e})
denote the external tensor structure corresponding to it under the bijection of
2.12
. Then
(\otimes,m^{i})
is geometric (in the sense of
7.4
)if and only if
(\boxtimes,m^{e})
is geometric (in the sense of
7.5
).

Proof.

In view of
7.6
the statement of the proposition makes sense, and it suffices to check that the constructions of
2.10
and
2.11
between internal and external tensor structures preserve the property of being geometric. By symmetry, it suffices to show that the invertibility of (
7
) implies that of (
8
) and conversely.

Let
(\otimes,m)
be a geometric internal tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}
, and let
(\boxtimes,m^{e})
denote the external tensor structure obtained from it via
2.10
. By construction, the natural transformation (
8
) associated to
(\boxtimes,m^{e})
is the left vertical composite in the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(p_{1}\times\textup{id}_{S_{2}})_{\#}(pr^{12*}_{1}A_{1}\otimes pr^{12*}_{2}A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(p_{1}\times\textup{id}_{S_{2}})_{\#}(pr^{12*}_{1}A_{1}\otimes(p_{1}\times\textup{id}_{S_{2}})^{*}pr^{12*}_{2}A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(p_{1}\times\textup{id}_{S_{2}})_{\#}(pr^{12*}_{1}p_{1}^{*}p_{1,\#}A_{1}\otimes pr^{12*}_{2}A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(p_{1}\times\textup{id}_{S_{2}})_{\#}((p_{1}\times\textup{id}_{S_{2}})^{*}(p_{1}\times\textup{id}_{S_{2}})_{\#}pr^{12*}_{1}A_{1}\otimes(p_{1}\times\textup{id}_{S_{2}})^{*}pr^{12*}_{2}A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(p_{1}\times\textup{id}_{S_{2}})_{\#}((p_{1}\times\textup{id}_{S_{2}})^{*}pr^{12*}_{1}p_{1,\#}A_{1}\otimes(p_{1}\times\textup{id}_{S_{2}})^{*}pr^{12*}_{2}A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(p_{1}\times\textup{id}_{S_{2}})_{\#}(p_{1}\times\textup{id}_{S_{2}})^{*}((p_{1}\times\textup{id}_{S_{2}})_{\#}pr^{12*}_{1}A_{1}\otimes(p_{1}\times\textup{id}_{S_{2}})^{*}pr^{12*}_{2}A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(p_{1}\times\textup{id}_{S_{2}})_{\#}(p_{1}\times\textup{id}_{S_{2}})^{*}(pr^{12*}_{1}p_{1,\#}A_{1}\otimes pr^{12*}_{2}A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((p_{1}\times\textup{id}_{S_{2}})_{\#}pr^{12*}_{1}A_{1}\otimes(p_{1}\times\textup{id}_{S_{2}})^{*}pr^{12*}_{2}A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(pr^{12*}_{1}p_{1,\#}A_{1}\otimes pr^{12*}_{2}A_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

where the three diagonal natural isomorphisms are induced by the natural isomorphism between functors
\mathbb{H}(P_{1})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S_{1}\times S_{2})

(p_{1}\times\textup{id}_{S_{2}})_{\#}pr^{12*}_{1}A_{1}\xrightarrow{\sim}pr^{12*}_{1}p_{1,\#}A_{1}

obtained by applying axiom (
\mathscr{S}^{sm}
-1) to the Cartesian square in
\mathscr{S}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{1}\times S_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{1}\times S_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{1}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}\times\textup{id}_{S_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12}_{1}}

Since the right vertical composite in the above diagram is invertible by definition (as
(\otimes,m)
is assumed to be geometric), the left vertical composite must be invertible as well.

Conversely, let
(\boxtimes,m)
be a geometric external tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}
, and let
(\otimes,m^{i})
denote the internal tensor structure obtained from it via
2.11
. By construction, the composite natural transformation (

) associated to
(\otimes,m^{i})
is the left side of the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\#}\Delta_{P}^{*}(A\boxtimes p^{*}B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\#}\Delta_{P}^{*}(\textup{id}_{P}\times p)^{*}(A\boxtimes B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\#}(\textup{id}_{P},p)^{*}(A\boxtimes B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\#}\Delta_{P}^{*}(p^{*}p_{\#}A\boxtimes p^{*}B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\#}\Delta_{P}^{*}(\textup{id}_{P}\times p)^{*}(p^{*}p_{\#}A\boxtimes B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\#}(\textup{id}_{P},p)^{*}(p^{*}p_{\#}A\boxtimes B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}(p\times\textup{id}_{S})_{\#}(A\boxtimes B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\#}\Delta_{P}^{*}(p\times p)^{*}(p_{\#}A\boxtimes B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\#}(\textup{id}_{P},p)^{*}(p\times\textup{id}_{S})^{*}(p_{\#}A\boxtimes B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}(p\times\textup{id}_{S})_{\#}(p^{*}p_{\#}A\boxtimes B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\#}p^{*}\Delta_{S}^{*}(p_{\#}A\boxtimes B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}(p\times\textup{id}_{S})_{\#}(p\times\textup{id}_{S})^{*}(p_{\#}A\boxtimes B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}(p_{\#}A\boxtimes B)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon}

where the three diagonal natural isomorphisms are induced by the natural isomorphism between functors
\mathbb{H}(P\times S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S)

p_{\#}(\textup{id}_{P},p)^{*}A\xrightarrow{\sim}\Delta_{S}^{*}(p\times\textup{id}_{S})_{\#}A

obtained by applying axiom (
\mathscr{S}^{sm}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\textup{id}_{P},p)}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p\times\textup{id}_{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}}

Since the right vertical composite in the above diagram is invertible by definition (as

is assumed to be geometric), the composite on the left side must be invertible as well.
∎

8.
Tensor structures on morphisms of fibered categories

Throughout this section, we fix two
\mathscr{S}
-fibered categories
\mathbb{H}_{1}
and
\mathbb{H}_{2}
as well as a morphism of
\mathscr{S}
-fibered categories
R:\mathbb{H}_{1}\rightarrow\mathbb{H}_{2}
; following the notation of
Section

1
, for every morphism
f:T\rightarrow S
in
\mathscr{S}
we let
\theta=\theta_{f}:f^{*}\circ R_{S}\xrightarrow{\sim}R_{T}\circ f^{*}
denote the corresponding
R
-transition isomorphism.

We are interested in studying how the notions introduced in the previous sections behave under the morphism
R
. To this end, we introduce analogues of tensor structures and their constraints for morphisms, both in the internal and in the external setting, and we compare them. The final outcome will be our second main result:

Theorem 8.1
.

For
j=1,2
, let
(\otimes_{j},m_{j}^{i})
be a (unitary, symmetric, associative) internal tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}_{j}
, and let
(\boxtimes_{j},m_{j}^{e})
denote the (unitary, symmetric, associative) external tensor structure corresponding to it under the bijection of
6.1
.

Then there exist canonical bijections between (unitary, symmetric, associative) internal tensor structures on
R
(with respect to
(\otimes_{1},m_{1}^{i})
and
(\otimes_{2},m_{2}^{i})
) and (unitary, symmetric, associative) external tensor structures on
R
(with respect to
(\boxtimes_{1},m_{1}^{e})
and
(\boxtimes_{2},m_{2}^{e})
).

By analogy with the case of tensor structures on single fibered categories, the first step of the proof consists in constructing explicit mutually inverse bijections between internal and external tensor structures on
R
: this is achieved in the first subsection. The second step is to show that such a bijection respects each possible additional constraint: this is achieved in the next three subsections.
As the reader will easily recognize, both the definitions and the arguments collected below are formally analogous to those of the previous sections.

8.1.
Internal and external tensor structures on morphisms

We start by introducing the notion of internal tensor structure on the morphism
R
. This is the natural extension of the usual notion of monoidal morphism between monoidal categories to the fibered setting.

Definition 8.2
.

Let
(\otimes_{1},m_{1})
and
(\otimes_{2},m_{2})
be internal tensor structures on
\mathbb{H}_{1}
and
\mathbb{H}_{2}
, respectively.
An
internal tensor structure

\rho
on
R
(with respect to
(\otimes_{1},m_{1})
and
(\otimes_{2},m_{2})
) is the datum of

•

for every
S\in\mathscr{S}
, a natural isomorphism of functors
\mathbb{H}_{1}(S)\times\mathbb{H}_{1}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}_{2}(S)

\rho=\rho_{S}:R_{S}(A)\otimes_{2}R_{S}(B)\xrightarrow{\sim}R_{S}(A\otimes_{1}B),

called the
internal
R
-monoidality isomorphism
at
S

satisfying the following condition:

(mor-ITS)

For every morphism
f:T\rightarrow S
in
\mathscr{S}
, the natural diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}_{1}(S)\times\mathbb{H}_{1}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}_{2}(T)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}R_{S}(A)\otimes_{2}f^{*}R_{S}(B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}(R_{S}(A)\otimes_{2}R_{S}(B))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}R_{S}(A\otimes_{1}B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{T}(f^{*}A)\otimes_{2}R_{T}(f^{*}B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{T}(f^{*}A\otimes_{1}f^{*}B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{T}(f^{*}(A\otimes_{1}B))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{1}}

is commutative.

The meaning of axiom (mor-ITS) is explained by the following result:

Lemma 8.3
.

For
j=1,2
let
(\otimes_{j},m_{j})
be an internal tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}_{j}
; let
f:T\rightarrow S
be a morphism in
\mathscr{S}
. For every composable triple of composable tuples in
\mathscr{S}

\underline{g}=(g_{1},\dots,g_{r}),\quad\underline{h}=(h_{1},\dots,h_{s}),\quad\underline{l}=(l_{i},\dots,l_{t})

satisfying
l_{t}\circ\dots\circ l_{1}\circ h_{s}\circ\dots\circ h_{1}\circ g_{r}\circ\dots\circ g_{1}=f
, consider the two functors

F^{\otimes_{1},R}_{(\underline{g},\underline{h},\underline{l})},\>F^{\otimes_{2},R}_{(\underline{g},\underline{h},\underline{l})}:\mathbb{H}_{1}(S)\times\mathbb{H}_{1}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}_{2}(T)

defined by the formulas

F^{\otimes_{1},R}_{(\underline{g},\underline{h},\underline{l})}(A,B):=\underline{g}^{*}R_{T^{\prime}}(\underline{h}^{*}(\underline{l}^{*}A\otimes_{1}\underline{l}^{*}B))

and

F^{\otimes_{2},R}_{(\underline{g},\underline{h},\underline{l})}(A,B):=\underline{g}^{*}(\underline{h}^{*}R_{T^{\prime\prime}}(\underline{l}^{*}A)\otimes_{2}\underline{h}^{*}R_{T^{\prime\prime}}(\underline{l}^{*}B)),

where
T^{\prime}
and
T^{\prime\prime}
denote the domains of
h_{1}
and
l_{1}
, respectively.

Then, for any two given composable triples of composable tuples
(\underline{g}^{(1)},\underline{h}^{(1)},\underline{l}^{(1)})
and
(\underline{g}^{(2)},\underline{h}^{(2)},\underline{l}^{(2)})
as above, and for each choice of
\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}\in\left\{1,2\right\}
, all possible isomorphisms of functors
\mathbb{H}_{1}(S)\times\mathbb{H}_{1}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}_{2}(T)

F^{\otimes_{\epsilon_{1}},R}_{(\underline{g}^{(1)},\underline{h}^{(1)},\underline{l}^{(1)})}\xrightarrow{\sim}F^{\otimes_{\epsilon_{2}},R}_{(\underline{g}^{(2)},\underline{h}^{(2)},\underline{l}^{(2)})}

obtained by composing connection isomorphisms, internal monoidality isomorphisms, connection
R
-isomorphisms and internal
R
-monoidality isomorphisms (and inverses thereof) coincide.

Proving this result directly would be extremely intricate, due to the presence of four different types of natural isomorphisms. To circumvent the difficulty, we use a variant of
2.5
based on
1.10
:

Construction 8.4
.

Keep the notation of
2.4
. Consider the two
(\mathscr{S}\times\mathscr{I})
-fibered categories
\mathbb{H}_{1}^{\otimes_{1}}
and
\mathbb{H}_{2}^{\otimes_{2}}
obtained via
2.5
. Then, following
1.9
, we can define a morphism of
(\mathscr{S}\times\mathscr{I})
-fibered categories
R^{\rho}:\mathbb{H}_{1}^{\otimes_{1}}\rightarrow\mathbb{H}_{2}^{\otimes_{2}}
as follows:

•

For every
S\in\mathscr{S}
, we set
R^{\rho}_{(S;1)}(A,B):=(R_{S}(A),R_{S}(B))
and
R^{\rho}_{(S;2)}(A):=R_{S}(A)
.

•

Given a morphism
\phi
in
\mathscr{S}\times\mathscr{I}
, we define the
R^{\rho}
-transition isomorphism
\theta_{\phi}
according to the type of
\phi
:

–

if
\phi=(f;1):(T;1)\rightarrow(S;1)
, we set

\theta_{\phi}(A):(f^{*}R_{S}(A),f^{*}R_{S}(B))\xrightarrow{(\theta_{f},\theta_{f})}(R_{T}(f^{*}A),R_{T}(f^{*}B));

–

if
\phi=(f;2):(T;2)\rightarrow(S;2)
, we set

\theta_{\phi}(A):f^{*}R_{S}(A)\xrightarrow{\theta_{f}}R_{T}(f^{*}A);

–

if
\phi=r_{S}\circ(f;2)
, we set

\theta_{\phi}(A,B):f^{*}(R_{S}(A)\otimes_{2}R_{S}(B))\xrightarrow{\rho_{S}}f^{*}R_{S}(A\otimes_{1}B).

Let us check that this construction indeed defines a morphism of
(\mathscr{S}\times\mathscr{I})
-fibered categories: given two composable morphisms
\phi:\mathsf{T}\rightarrow\mathsf{S}
and
\psi:\mathsf{S}\rightarrow\mathsf{V}
in
\mathscr{S}\times\mathscr{I}
, we have to show that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\psi\phi)^{*}R^{\rho}_{\mathsf{V}}(\mathsf{A})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R^{\rho}_{\mathsf{T}}((\psi\phi)^{*}\mathsf{A})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi^{*}\psi^{*}R^{\rho}_{\mathsf{V}}(\mathsf{A})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi^{*}R^{\rho}_{\mathsf{S}}(\psi^{*}\mathsf{A})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R^{\rho}_{\mathsf{T}}(\phi^{*}\psi^{*}\mathsf{A})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta^{\rho}_{\psi\phi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta^{\rho}_{\phi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta^{\rho}_{\psi}}

is commutative. We have the following possibilities, indexed by pairs of composable morphisms
f:T\rightarrow S
and
g:S\rightarrow V
in
\mathscr{S}
:

(1)

If
\phi=(f;1)
and
\psi=(g;1)
, we obtain the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((gf)^{*}R_{V}(A),(gf)^{*}R_{V}(B))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(R_{T}((gf)^{*}A),R_{T}((gf)^{*}B))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f^{*}g^{*}R_{V}(A),f^{*}g^{*}R_{V}(B))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f^{*}R_{S}(g^{*}A),f^{*}R_{S}(g^{*}B))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(R_{T}(f^{*}g^{*}A),R_{T}(f^{*}g^{*}B))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\theta,\theta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\theta,\theta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\theta,\theta)}

which is commutative by axiom (mor-
\mathscr{S}
-fib).

(2)

Similarly, if
\phi=(f;2)
and
\psi=(g;2)
, we obtain the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(gf)^{*}R_{V}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{T}((gf)^{*}A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}g^{*}R_{V}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}R_{S}(g^{*}A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{T}(f^{*}g^{*}A)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}

which is commutative by axiom (mor-
\mathscr{S}
-fib).

(3)

If
\phi=(f;2)
and
\psi=r_{V}\circ(g;2)
, we obtain the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(gf)^{*}(R_{V}(A)\otimes_{2}R_{V}(B))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(gf)^{*}R_{V}(A\otimes_{1}B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{T}((gf)^{*}(A\otimes_{1}B))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}g^{*}(R_{V}(A)\otimes_{2}R_{V}(B))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}g^{*}R_{V}(A\otimes_{1}B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}R_{S}(g^{*}(A\otimes_{1}B))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{T}(f^{*}g^{*}(A\otimes_{1}B))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}

where the left-most piece is commutative by naturality while the right-most piece is commutative by axiom (mor-
\mathscr{S}
-fib).

(4)

Finally, if
\phi=r_{S}\circ(f;2)
and
\psi=(g;1)
, we obtain the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(gf)^{*}(R_{V}(A)\otimes_{2}R_{V}(B))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(gf)^{*}R_{V}(A\otimes_{1}B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{T}((gf)^{*}(A\otimes_{1}B))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}g^{*}(R_{V}(A)\otimes_{2}R_{V}(B))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}g^{*}R_{V}(A\otimes_{1}B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}R_{S}(g^{*}(A\otimes_{1}B))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{T}(f^{*}g^{*}(A\otimes_{1}B))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}(g^{*}R_{V}(A)\otimes_{2}g^{*}R_{V}(B))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}(R_{S}(g^{*}A)\otimes_{2}R_{S}(g^{*}B))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}R_{S}(g^{*}A\otimes_{1}g^{*}B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{T}(f^{*}(g^{*}A\otimes_{1}g^{*}B))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{1}}

where the two squares are commutative by naturality, the upper-right rectangle is commutative by axiom (mor-
\mathscr{S}
-fib), while the lower-left rectangle is commutative by axiom (mor-ITS).

Proof of
8.3
.

As a consequence of
8.4
, this is just a particular case of
1.8
.
∎

In a similar way, we introduce the notion of external tensor structure on the morphism
R
.

Definition 8.5
.

Let
(\boxtimes_{1},m_{1})
and
(\boxtimes_{2},m_{2})
be external tensor structures on
\mathbb{H}_{1}
and
\mathbb{H}_{2}
, respectively.
An
external tensor structure

\rho
on
R
(with respect to
(\boxtimes_{1},m_{1})
and
(\boxtimes_{2},m_{2})
) is the datum of

•

for every
S_{1},S_{2}\in\mathscr{S}
, a natural isomorphism of functors
\mathbb{H}_{1}(S_{1})\times\mathbb{H}_{1}(S_{2})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}_{2}(S_{1}\times S_{2})

\rho=\rho_{S_{1},S_{2}}:R_{S_{1}}(A_{1})\boxtimes_{2}R_{S_{2}}(A_{2})\xrightarrow{\sim}R_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}(A_{1}\boxtimes_{1}A_{2}),

called the
external
R
-monoidality isomorphism
at
(S_{1},S_{2})
,

satisfying the following condition:

(mor-ETS)

For every two morphisms
f_{i}:T_{i}\rightarrow S_{i}
in
\mathscr{S}
,
i=1,2
, the natural diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}_{1}(S_{1})\times\mathbb{H}_{1}(S_{2})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}_{2}(T_{1}\times T_{2})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}^{*}R_{S_{1}}(A_{1})\boxtimes_{2}f_{2}^{*}R_{S_{2}}(A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}(R_{S_{1}}(A_{1})\boxtimes_{2}R_{S_{2}}(A_{2}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}R_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}(A_{1}\boxtimes_{1}A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{T_{1}}(f_{1}^{*}A_{1})\boxtimes_{2}R_{T_{2}}(f_{2}^{*}A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{T_{1}\times T_{2}}(f_{1}^{*}A_{1}\boxtimes_{1}f_{2}^{*}A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{T_{1}\times T_{2}}((f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}(A_{1}\boxtimes_{1}A_{2}))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{1}}

is commutative.

As the reader may expect, we have the following analogue of
2.4
:

Lemma 8.6
.

For
j=1,2
let
(\boxtimes_{j},m_{j})
be an external tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}_{j}
and, for
i=1,2
, let
f_{i}:T_{i}\rightarrow S_{i}
be a morphism in
\mathscr{S}
. Given, for
i=1,2
, a composable triple of composable tuples in
\mathscr{S}

\underline{g}_{i}=(g_{i,1},\dots,g_{i,r}),\quad\underline{h}_{i}=(h_{i,1},\dots,h_{i,s}),\quad\underline{l}_{i}=(l_{i,1},\dots,l_{i,t})

satisfying
l_{i,t}\circ\dots\circ l_{i,1}\circ h_{i,s}\circ\dots\circ h_{i,1}\circ g_{i,r}\circ\dots\circ g_{i,1}=f_{i}
, consider the two functors

F^{\boxtimes_{1},R}_{(\underline{g}_{1},\underline{h}_{1},\underline{l}_{1}),(\underline{g}_{2},\underline{h}_{2},\underline{l}_{2})},\>F^{\boxtimes_{2},R}_{(\underline{g}_{1},\underline{h}_{1},\underline{l}_{1}),(\underline{g}_{2},\underline{h}_{2},\underline{l}_{2})}:\mathbb{H}_{1}(S_{1})\times\mathbb{H}_{1}(S_{2})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}_{2}(T_{1}\times T_{2})

defined by the formulas

F^{\boxtimes_{1},R}_{(\underline{g}_{1},\underline{h}_{1},\underline{l}_{1}),(\underline{g}_{2},\underline{h}_{2},\underline{l}_{2})}(A_{1},A_{2}):=(\underline{g}_{1}\times\underline{g}_{2})^{*}R_{T^{\prime}_{1}\times T^{\prime}_{2}}((\underline{h}_{1}\times\underline{h}_{2})^{*}(\underline{l}_{1}^{*}A_{1}\boxtimes_{1}\underline{l}_{2}^{*}A_{2}))

and

F^{\boxtimes_{2},R}_{(\underline{g}_{1},\underline{h}_{1},\underline{l}_{1}),(\underline{g}_{2},\underline{h}_{2},\underline{l}_{2})}(A_{1},A_{2}):=(\underline{g}_{1}\times\underline{g}_{2})^{*}(\underline{h}_{1}^{*}R_{T^{\prime\prime}_{1}}(\underline{l}_{1}^{*}A_{1})\boxtimes_{2}\underline{h}_{2}^{*}R_{T^{\prime\prime}_{2}}(\underline{l}_{2}^{*}A_{2}))

where, for
i=1,2
,
T^{\prime}_{i}
and
T^{\prime\prime}_{i}
denote the domain of
h_{i,1}
and
l_{i,1}
, respectively.

Then, for any two given composable triples of composable tuples
(\underline{g}_{i}^{(1)},\underline{h}_{i}^{(1)},\underline{l}_{i}^{(1)})
and
(\underline{g}_{i}^{(2)},\underline{h}_{i}^{(2)},\underline{l}_{i}^{(2)})
as above,
i=1,2
, and for any choice of numbers
\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}\in\left\{1,2\right\}
, all isomorphisms of functors
\mathbb{H}_{1}(S_{1})\times\mathbb{H}_{1}(S_{2})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}_{2}(T_{1}\times T_{2})

F^{\boxtimes_{\epsilon_{1}},R}_{(\underline{g}_{1}^{(1)},\underline{h}_{1}^{(1)},\underline{l}_{1}^{(1)}),(\underline{g}_{2}^{(1)},\underline{h}_{2}^{(1)},\underline{l}_{2}^{(1)})}(A_{1},A_{2})\xrightarrow{\sim}F^{\boxtimes_{\epsilon_{2}},R}_{(\underline{g}_{1}^{(2)},\underline{h}_{1}^{(2)},\underline{l}_{1}^{(2)}),(\underline{g}_{2}^{(2)},\underline{h}_{2}^{(2)},\underline{l}_{2}^{(2)})}(A_{1},A_{2})

obtained by composing connection isomorphisms, external monoidality isomorphisms,
R
-transition isomorphisms and external
R
-monoidality isomorphisms (and inverses thereof) coincide.

Construction 8.7
.

Keep the notation of
8.6
. Consider the two
(\mathscr{S}^{2}\times\mathscr{I})
-fibered categories
\mathbb{H}_{1}^{\boxtimes_{1}}
and
\mathbb{H}_{2}^{\boxtimes_{2}}
obtained via Construction
2.9
. Then, following
1.9
, we can define a morphism of
(\mathscr{S}^{2}\times\mathscr{I})
-fibered categories
R^{\rho}:\mathbb{H}_{1}^{\boxtimes_{1}}\rightarrow\mathbb{H}_{2}^{\boxtimes_{2}}
as follows:

•

For every
S_{1},S_{2}\in\mathscr{S}
, we set
R^{\rho}_{(S_{1},S_{2};1)}(A_{1},A_{2}):=(R_{S_{1}}(A_{1}),R_{S_{2}}(A_{2}))
and
R^{\rho}_{(S_{1},S_{2};2)}(A):=R_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}(A)
.

•

Given a morphism
\phi
in
\mathscr{S}^{2}\times\mathscr{I}
, we define the
R^{\rho}
-transition isomorphism
\theta_{\phi}
according to the type of
\phi
:

–

if
\phi=(f_{1},f_{2};1):(T_{1},T_{2};1)\rightarrow(S_{1},S_{2};1)
, we set

\theta_{\phi}(A):(f_{1}^{*}R_{S_{1}}(A_{1}),f_{2}^{*}R_{S_{2}}(A_{2}))\xrightarrow{(\theta_{f},\theta_{f})}(R_{T_{1}}(f_{1}^{*}A_{1}),R_{T_{2}}(f_{2}^{*}A_{2}));

–

if
\phi=(f_{1},f_{2};2):(T_{1},T_{2};2)\rightarrow(S_{1},S_{2};2)
, we set

\theta_{\phi}(A):(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}R_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}(A_{1})\xrightarrow{\theta_{f_{1}\times f_{2}}}R_{T_{1}\times T_{2}}((f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}A);

–

if
\phi=r_{S_{1},S_{2}}\circ(f_{1},f_{2};2)
, we set

\theta_{\phi}(A_{1},A_{2}):(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}(R_{S_{1}}(A_{1})\boxtimes_{2}R_{S_{2}}(A_{2}))\xrightarrow{\rho_{S_{1},S_{2}}}(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}R_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}(A_{1}\boxtimes_{1}A_{2}).

Arguing in the same way as in
8.4
, one can check that this construction indeed defines a morphism of
(\mathscr{S}^{2}\times\mathscr{I})
-fibered categories; we omit the details.

Proof of
8.6
.

As a consequence of
8.7
, this is just a particular case of
1.8
.
∎

Remark 8.8
.

In the following, we consider
\mathscr{S}
-fibered categories
\mathbb{H}_{1}
,
\mathbb{H}_{2}
and
\mathbb{H}_{3}
as well as morphisms of
\mathscr{S}
-fibered categories
R_{1}:\mathbb{H}_{1}\rightarrow\mathbb{H}_{2}
and
R_{2}:\mathbb{H}_{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{H}_{3}
.

(1)

Suppose that
\mathbb{H}_{1}=\mathbb{H}_{2}=\mathbb{H}
and
R_{1}=\textup{id}_{\mathbb{H}}
. By definition, an internal tensor structure on
\textup{id}_{\mathbb{H}}
with respect to two given internal tensor structures
(\otimes^{\prime},m^{\prime})
and
(\otimes,m)
is the same as an equivalence of internal tensor structures
e:(\otimes,m)\xrightarrow{\sim}(\otimes^{\prime},m^{\prime})
.

(2)

For
j=1,2,3
let
(\otimes_{j},m_{j})
be an internal tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}_{j}
.
Given internal tensor structures
\rho_{1}
on
R_{1}
(with respect to
\otimes_{1}
and
\otimes_{2}
) and
\rho_{2}
on
R_{2}
(with respect to
\otimes_{2}
and
\otimes_{3}
), we obtain an obvious composite internal tensor structure
\rho_{2}\circ\rho_{1}
on the composite morphism
R_{2}\circ R_{1}
(with respect to
\otimes_{1}
and
\otimes_{3}
).

(3)

For
j=1,2
let
e_{j}:(\otimes_{j},m_{j})\xrightarrow{\sim}(\otimes^{\prime}_{j},m^{\prime}_{j})
be an equivalence of internal tensor structures on
\mathbb{H}_{j}
, for simplicity, set
R:=R_{1}
.
Combining the previous two observations, we see that the pair
(e_{1},e_{2})
determines a canonical bijection between internal tensor structures on
R
with respect to
(\otimes_{1},m_{1})
and
(\otimes_{2},m_{2})
and internal tensor structures on
R
with respect to
(\otimes^{\prime}_{1},m^{\prime}_{1})
and
(\otimes^{\prime}_{2},m^{\prime}_{2})
.
In practice, this means that every question concerning the existence of internal tensor structures on the morphism
R
only depends on the equivalence classes of the chosen internal tensor structures on
\mathbb{H}_{1}
and
\mathbb{H}_{2}
in a very precise way.

Analogous considerations hold in the setting of external tensor structures.

The following two results explain how to translate an internal tensor structure on
R
into an external tensor structure on it and conversely:

Lemma 8.9
.

For
j=1,2
, let
(\otimes_{j},m_{j})
be an internal tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}_{j}
, and let
(\boxtimes_{j},m_{j}^{e})
denote the external tensor structure obtained form it via
2.10
.
Suppose that we are given an internal tensor structure
\rho
on
R
(with respect to
(\otimes_{1},m_{1})
and
(\otimes_{2},m_{2})
). Then, associating

•

to every
S_{1},S_{2}\in\mathscr{S}
,the natural isomorphism of functors
\mathbb{H}_{1}(S_{1})\times\mathbb{H}_{1}(S_{2})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}_{2}(S_{1}\times S_{2})

\rho^{e}=\rho^{e}_{S_{1},S_{2}}:R_{S_{1}}(A_{1})\boxtimes_{2}R_{S_{2}}(A_{2})\xrightarrow{\sim}R_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}(A_{1}\boxtimes_{1}A_{2})

defined by taking the composite

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{1}}(A_{1})\boxtimes_{2}R_{S_{2}}(A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}(A_{1}\boxtimes_{1}A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{1}R_{S_{1}}(A_{1})\otimes_{2}pr^{12,*}_{2}R_{S_{2}}(A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}(pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1})\otimes_{2}R_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}(pr^{12,*}_{2}A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}(pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1}\otimes_{1}pr^{12,*}_{2}A_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}

defines an external tensor structure
\rho^{e}
on
R
(with respect to
(\boxtimes_{1},m_{1}^{e})
and
(\boxtimes_{2},m_{2}^{e})
).

Proof.

We need to check that the definition satisfies condition (mor-ETS): given morphisms
f_{i}:T_{1}\rightarrow S_{i}
in
\mathscr{S}
,
i=1,2
, we have to show that the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S_{1})\times\mathbb{H}(S_{2})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(T_{1}\times T_{2})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}^{*}R_{S_{1}}(A_{1})\boxtimes_{2}f_{2}^{*}R_{S_{2}}(A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}(R_{S_{1}}(A_{1})\boxtimes_{2}R_{S_{2}}(A_{2}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}R_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}(A_{1}\boxtimes_{1}A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{T_{1}}(f_{1}^{*}A_{1})\boxtimes_{2}R_{T_{2}}(f_{2}^{*}A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{T_{1}\times T_{2}}(f_{1}^{*}A_{1}\boxtimes_{1}f_{2}^{*}A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{T_{1}\times T_{2}}((f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}(A_{1}\boxtimes_{1}A_{2}))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{2}^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{1}^{e}}

is commutative. To this end, we decompose it as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{12}^{*}R_{S_{12}}(B_{1})\otimes_{2}f_{12}^{*}R_{S_{12}}(B_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{12}^{*}(R_{S_{12}}(B_{1})\otimes_{2}R_{S_{12}}(B_{2}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{12}^{*}R_{S_{12}}(B_{1}\otimes_{1}B_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{T_{12}}(f_{12}^{*}B_{1})\otimes_{2}R_{T_{12}}(f_{12}^{*}B_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{T_{12}}(f_{12}^{*}B_{1}\otimes_{1}(f_{12})^{*}B_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{T_{12}}(f_{12}^{*}(B_{1}\otimes_{1}B_{2}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{2}^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{e}}

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{1}^{e}}

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

where, for notational convenience, we write
B_{r}:=pr^{12,*}_{r}A_{r}
,
i=1,2
. The six unnamed isomorphisms are obtained by combining connection isomorphisms, internal monoidality isomorphisms and
R
-isomorphisms along suitable paths; by
8.3
, we know that the definitions are independent of the chosen paths. Since the central rectangle in the latter diagram is commutative by axiom (mor-ITS), it now suffices to show that the six lateral pieces are commutative as well. This follows by the usual trick; we omit the details.
∎

Lemma 8.10
.

For
j=1,2
, let
(\boxtimes_{j},m_{j})
be an internal tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}_{j}
, and let
(\otimes_{j},m_{j}^{i})
denote the external tensor structure obtained form it via
2.11
.
Suppose that we are given an external tensor structure
\rho
on
R
(with respect to
(\boxtimes_{1},m_{1})
and
(\boxtimes_{2},m_{2})
). Then, associating

•

to every
S\in\mathscr{S}
, the natural isomorphism of functors
\mathbb{H}_{1}(S)\times\mathbb{H}_{1}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}_{2}(S)

(9)

\rho^{i}=\rho^{i}_{S}:R_{S}(A)\otimes_{2}R_{S}(B)\xrightarrow{\sim}R_{S}(A\otimes_{1}B)

defined by taking the composite

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(A)\otimes_{2}R_{S}(B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(A\otimes_{1}B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}(R_{S}(A)\boxtimes_{2}R_{S}(B))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}R_{S}(A\boxtimes_{1}B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(\Delta_{S}^{*}(A\boxtimes_{1}B))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}

defines an internal tensor structure
\rho^{e}
on
R
(with respect to
(\otimes_{1},m_{1}^{i})
and
(\otimes_{2},m_{2}^{i})
).

Proof.

We need to check that the definition satisfies condition (mor-ITS): given a morphism
f:T\rightarrow S
in
\mathscr{S}
, we have to show that the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S)\times\mathbb{H}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(T)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}R_{S}(A)\otimes_{2}f^{*}R_{S}(B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}(R_{S}(A)\otimes_{2}R_{S}(B))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}R_{S}(A\otimes_{1}B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{T}(f^{*}A)\otimes_{2}R_{T}(f^{*}B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{T}(f^{*}A\otimes_{1}f^{*}B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{T}(f^{*}(A\otimes_{1}B))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{2}^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{1}^{i}}

is commutative. To this end, we decompose it as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{T}^{*}(f^{*}R_{S}(A)\boxtimes_{2}f^{*}R_{S}(B))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{T}^{*}(f\times f)^{*}(R_{S}(A)\boxtimes_{2}R_{S}(B))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{T}^{*}(f\times f)^{*}R_{S\times S}(A\boxtimes_{1}B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{T}^{*}(R_{T}(f^{*}A)\boxtimes_{2}R_{T}(f^{*}B))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{T}^{*}R_{T\times T}(f^{*}A\boxtimes_{1}f^{*}B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{T}^{*}R_{T\times T}((f\times f)^{*}(A\boxtimes_{1}B))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{2}^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{i}}

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{1}^{i}}

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

Here, the six unnamed natural isomorphisms are obtained by taking composites of connection isomorphisms, external monoidality isomorphisms and
R
-isomorphisms along suitable paths; by
8.6
, we know that the definitions are independent of the chosen paths. Since the central rectangle is commutative by axiom (mor-ETS), it now suffices to show that the six lateral pieces are commutative as well. This follows by the usual trick; we omit the details.
∎

Proposition 8.11
.

For
j=1,2
, let
(\otimes_{j},m_{j}^{i})
be an internal tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}_{i}
, and let
(\boxtimes_{j},m_{j}^{e})
denote the external tensor structure corresponding to it (modulo equivalence) via the bijection of
2.12
.
Then the constructions of
8.9
and
8.10
define mutually inverse bijections between internal tensor structures on
R
(with respect to
(\otimes_{1},m_{1}^{i})
and
(\otimes_{2},m_{2}^{i})
) and external tensor structures on
R
(with respect to
(\boxtimes_{1},m_{1}^{e})
and
(\boxtimes_{2},m_{2}^{e})
).

Proof.

Start from two internal tensor structures
(\otimes_{1},m_{1})
and
(\otimes_{2},m_{2})
and an internal tensor structure
\rho
on
R
(with respect to
(\otimes_{1},m_{1})
and
(\otimes_{2},m_{2})
).
For
j=1,2
, let
(\otimes^{\prime}_{j},m^{\prime}_{j})
denote the internal tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}_{j}
obtained from
(\otimes_{j},m_{j})
by applying first
2.10
and then
2.11
; moreover, let
e_{j}:(\otimes_{j},m_{j})\xrightarrow{\sim}(\otimes^{\prime}_{j},m^{\prime}_{j})
denote the equivalence of internal tensor structures constructed in
2.12
.
Finally, let
\rho^{\prime}
denote the internal tensor structure on
R
(with respect to
(\otimes^{\prime}_{1},m^{\prime}_{1})
and
(\otimes^{\prime}_{2},m^{\prime}_{2})
) obtained from
\rho
by applying first
8.9
and then
8.10
.
We have to show that, for every
S\in\mathscr{S}
, the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}_{1}(S)\times\mathbb{H}_{1}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}_{2}(S)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(A)\otimes_{2}R_{S}(B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(A\otimes_{1}B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(A)\otimes^{\prime}_{2}R_{S}(B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(A\otimes^{\prime}_{1}B)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{\prime}}

is commutative. Unwinding the various constructions, we obtain the more explicit diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(A)\otimes_{2}R_{S}(B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(A\otimes_{1}B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}pr^{12,*}_{1}R_{S}(A)\otimes_{2}\Delta_{S}^{*}pr^{12,*}_{2}R_{S}(B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(\Delta_{S}^{*}pr^{12,*}_{1}A\otimes_{1}\Delta_{S}^{*}pr^{12,*}_{2}B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}(pr^{12,*}_{1}R_{S}(A)\otimes_{2}pr^{12,*}_{2}R_{S}(B))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(\Delta_{S}^{*}(pr^{12,*}_{1}A\otimes_{1}pr^{12,*}_{2}B))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}(R_{S\times S}(pr^{12,*}_{1}A)\otimes_{2}R_{S\times S}(pr^{12,*}_{2}B))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}R_{S\times S}(pr^{12,*}_{1}A\otimes_{1}pr^{12,*}_{2}B)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}

that we decompose as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(\Delta_{S}^{*}pr^{12,*}_{1}A)\otimes_{2}R_{S}(\Delta_{S}^{*}pr^{12,*}_{2}B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}R_{S\times S}(pr^{12,*}_{1}A)\otimes_{2}\Delta_{S}^{*}R_{S\times S}(pr^{12,*}_{2}B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}

Here, the lower-right piece is commutative by axiom (mor-ITS) while the other pieces are commutative by naturality and by axiom (mor-
\mathscr{S}
-fib). This proves the claim.

Conversely, start from two external tensor structures
(\boxtimes_{1},m_{1})
and
(\boxtimes_{2},m_{2})
and an external tensor structure
\rho
on
R
(with respect to
(\boxtimes_{1},m_{1})
and
(\boxtimes_{2},m_{2})
).
For
j=1,2
, let
(\boxtimes^{\prime}_{j},m^{\prime}_{j})
denote the external tensor structure obtained from
(\boxtimes_{j},m_{j})
by applying first
2.11
and then
2.10
; moreover, let
e_{j}:(\boxtimes^{\prime}_{j},m^{\prime}_{j})\xrightarrow{\sim}(\boxtimes_{j},m_{j})
denote the equivalence of external tensor structures constructed in
2.12
.
Finally, let
\rho^{\prime}
denote the external tensor structure on
R
(with respect to
(\boxtimes^{\prime}_{1},m^{\prime}_{1})
and
(\boxtimes^{\prime}_{2},m^{\prime}_{2})
) obtained from
\rho
by applying first

and then
8.9
.
We have to show that, for every
S_{1},S_{2}\in\mathscr{S}
, the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}_{1}(S_{1})\times\mathbb{H}_{1}(S_{2})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}_{2}(S_{1}\times S_{2})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{1}}(A_{1})\boxtimes_{2}R_{S_{2}}(A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}(A_{1}\boxtimes_{1}A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{1}}(A_{1})\boxtimes^{\prime}_{2}R_{S_{2}}(A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}(A_{1}\boxtimes^{\prime}_{1}A_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{1}}

is commutative. Unwinding the various constructions, we obtain the more explicit diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{1}}(A_{1})\boxtimes_{2}R_{S_{2}}(A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}(A_{1}\boxtimes_{1}A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}^{*}(pr^{12}_{1}\times pr^{12}_{2})^{*}(R_{S_{1}}(A_{1})\boxtimes_{2}R_{S_{2}}(A_{2}))}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}^{*}(pr^{12,*}_{1}R_{S_{1}}(A_{1})\boxtimes_{2}pr^{12,*}_{2}R_{S_{2}}(A_{2}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}(\Delta_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}^{*}(pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1}\boxtimes_{1}pr^{12,*}_{2}A_{2}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}^{*}(R_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}(pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1})\boxtimes_{2}R_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}(pr^{12,*}_{2}A_{2}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}^{*}R_{(S_{1}\times S_{2})^{2}}(pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1}\boxtimes_{1}pr^{12,*}_{2}A_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{1}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}

that we decompose as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{2}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{1}}
