Here, the lower-left piece is commutative by axiom (mor-ETS) while the other pieces are commutative by naturality and by axiom (mor-
\mathscr{S}
-fib). This proves the claim and concludes the proof.
∎

8.2.
Associative, symmetric, unitary morphisms

We now introduce the notions of associativity, commutativity and unitarity for internal and external tensor structures on morphisms, and we compare the two versions of each of them. This task is relatively easy since it does not involve constructing new structures but only amounts to checking some natural properties. This is done in
8.14
and
8.15
(associativity),
8.18
and
8.19
(symmetry),
8.22
and
8.23
(unitarity). Then
8.1
follows combining
8.11
with these complementary results.

Let us start by studying the notion of associativity for internal and external tensor structures on morphisms.

Definition 8.12
.

Let
(\otimes_{1},m_{1})
and
(\otimes_{2},m_{2})
be internal tensor structures on
\mathbb{H}_{1}
and
\mathbb{H}_{2}
, respectively; let
\rho
be an internal tensor structure on
R
(with respect to
(\otimes_{1},m_{1})
and
(\otimes_{2},m_{2})
).

Suppose that
a_{1}
and
a_{2}
are associativity constraints on
(\otimes_{1},m_{1})
and
(\otimes_{2},m_{2})
, respectively. We say that
\rho
is
associative
(with respect to
a_{1}
and
a_{2}
) if it satisfies the following additional condition:

(mor-
a
ITS)

For every
S\in\mathscr{S}
, the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}_{1}(S)\times\mathbb{H}_{1}(S)\times\mathbb{H}_{1}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}_{2}(S)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(R_{S}(A)\otimes_{2}R_{S}(B))\otimes_{2}R_{S}(C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(A\otimes_{1}B)\otimes_{2}R_{S}(C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}((A\otimes_{1}B)\otimes_{1}C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(A)\otimes_{2}(R_{S}(B)\otimes_{2}R_{S}(C))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(A)\otimes_{2}R_{S}(B\otimes_{1}C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(A\otimes_{1}(B\otimes_{1}C))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}

is commutative.

Definition 8.13
.

Let
(\boxtimes_{1},m_{1})
and
(\boxtimes_{2},m_{2})
be external tensor structures on
\mathbb{H}_{1}
and
\mathbb{H}_{2}
, respectively; let
\rho
be an external tensor structure on
R
(with respect to
(\boxtimes_{1},m_{1})
and
(\boxtimes_{2},m_{2})
).

Let
a_{1}
and
a_{2}
be external associativity constraints on
(\boxtimes_{1},m_{1})
and
(\boxtimes_{2},m_{2})
, respectively. We say that
\rho
is
associative
(with respect to
a_{1}
and
a_{2}
) if it satisfies the following additional condition:

(mor-
a
ETS)

For every
S_{1},S_{2},S_{3}\in\mathscr{S}
, the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}_{1}(S_{1})\times\mathbb{H}_{1}(S_{2})\times\mathbb{H}_{1}(S_{3})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}_{2}(S_{1}\times S_{2}\times S_{3})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(R_{S_{1}}(A_{1})\boxtimes R_{S_{2}}(A_{2}))\boxtimes R_{S_{3}}(A_{3})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}(A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2})\boxtimes R_{S_{3}}(A_{3})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{1}\times S_{2}\times S_{3}}((A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2})\boxtimes A_{3})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{1}}(A_{1})\boxtimes(R_{S_{2}}(A_{2})\boxtimes R_{S_{3}}(A_{3}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{1}}(A_{1})\boxtimes R_{S_{2}\times S_{2}}(A_{2}\boxtimes A_{3})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{1}\times S_{2}\times S_{3}}(A_{1}\boxtimes(A_{2}\boxtimes A_{3}))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}

is commutative.

Lemma 8.14
.

For
j=1,2
let
(\otimes_{j},m_{j};a_{j})
be an associative internal tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}_{j}
, and let
(\boxtimes_{j},m_{j}^{e};a_{j}^{e})
denote the associative external tensor structure obtained from it via
2.10
and
3.3
.
Moreover, let
\rho
be an internal tensor structure on
R
(with respect to
(\otimes_{1},m_{1})
and
(\otimes_{2},m_{2})
), and let
\rho^{e}
denote the corresponding external tensor structure on
R
(with respect to
(\boxtimes_{1},m_{1}^{e})
and
(\boxtimes_{2},m_{2}^{e})
).

Suppose that
\rho
is associative (with respect to
a_{1}
and
a_{2}
). Then
\rho^{e}
is associative (with respect to
a_{1}^{e}
and
a_{2}^{e}
).

Proof.

Let us check that the external tensor structure
\rho^{e}
satisfies condition (mor-
a
ETS): given
S_{1},S_{2},S_{3}\in\mathscr{S}
, we have to show that the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}_{1}(S_{1})\times\mathbb{H}_{1}(S_{2})\times\mathbb{H}_{1}(S_{3})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}_{2}(S_{1}\times S_{2}\times S_{3})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(R_{S_{1}}(A_{1})\boxtimes_{2}R_{S_{2}}(A_{2}))\boxtimes_{2}R_{S_{3}}(A_{3})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{12}}(A_{1}\boxtimes_{1}A_{2})\boxtimes_{2}R_{S_{3}}(A_{3})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{123}}((A_{1}\boxtimes_{1}A_{2})\boxtimes_{1}A_{3})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{1}}(A_{1})\boxtimes_{2}(R_{S_{2}}(A_{2})\boxtimes_{2}R_{S_{3}}(A_{3}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{1}}(A_{1})\boxtimes_{2}R_{S_{23}}(A_{2}\boxtimes_{1}A_{3})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{123}}(A_{1}\boxtimes_{1}(A_{2}\boxtimes_{1}A_{3}))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{2}^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{1}^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{e}}

is commutative. To this end, we decompose it as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(R_{S_{123}}(B_{1})\otimes_{2}R_{S_{123}}(B_{2}))\otimes_{2}R_{S_{123}}(B_{3})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{123}}(B_{1}\otimes_{1}B_{2})\otimes_{2}R_{S_{123}}(B_{3})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{123}}((B_{1}\otimes_{1}B_{2})\otimes_{1}B_{3})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{123}}(B_{1})\otimes_{2}(R_{S_{123}}(B_{2})\otimes_{2}R_{S_{123}}(B_{3}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{123}}(B_{1})\otimes_{2}R_{S_{123}}(B_{2}\otimes_{1}B_{3})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{123}}(B_{1}\otimes_{1}(B_{2}\otimes_{1}B_{3}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{2}^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{e}}

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{1}^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{e}}

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

where, for notational convenience, we have set
B_{i}:=pr^{123,*}_{i}A_{i}
,
i=1,2,3
. Here, the six unnamed natural isomorphisms are obtained by combining connection isomorphisms, internal monoidality isomorphisms and
R
-transition isomorphisms along suitable paths; by
8.3
, we know that the definitions do not depend on the chosen paths. Since the central rectangle in the latter diagram is commutative by axiom (mor-
a
ITS), it now suffices to show that the remaining six lateral pieces are commutative as well. This can be achieved by the usual trick; we omit the details.
∎

Lemma 8.15
.

For
j=1,2
let
(\boxtimes_{j},m_{j};a_{j})
be an associative external tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}_{j}
, and let
(\otimes_{j},m_{j}^{i};a_{j}^{i})
denote the associative internal tensor structure obtained from it via
2.11
and
3.4
.
Moreover, let
\rho
be an external tensor structure on
R
(with respect to
(\boxtimes_{1},m_{1})
and
(\boxtimes_{2},m_{2})
), and let
\rho^{i}
denote the corresponding internal tensor structure on
R
(with respect to
(\otimes_{1},m_{1}^{i})
and
(\otimes_{2},m_{2}^{i})
).

Suppose that
\rho
is associative (with respect to
a_{1}
and
a_{2}
). Then
\rho^{i}
is associative (with respect to
a_{1}^{i}
and
a_{2}^{i}
).

Proof.

Let us check that the internal tensor structure
\rho^{i}
satisfies condition (mor-
a
ITS): given
S\in\mathscr{S}
, we have to show that the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}_{1}(S)\times\mathbb{H}_{1}(S)\times\mathbb{H}_{1}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}_{2}(S)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(R_{S}(A)\otimes_{2}R_{S}(B))\otimes_{2}R_{S}(C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(A\otimes_{1}B)\otimes_{2}R_{S}(C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}((A\otimes_{1}B)\otimes_{1}C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(A)\otimes_{2}(R_{S}(B)\otimes_{2}R_{S}(C))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(A)\otimes_{2}R_{S}(B\otimes_{1}C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(A\otimes_{1}(B\otimes_{1}C))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{2}^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{1}^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{i}}

is commutative. To this end, we decompose it as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{(3),*}((R_{S}(A)\boxtimes R_{S}(B))\boxtimes R_{S}(C))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{(3),*}(R_{S\times S}(A\boxtimes B)\boxtimes R_{S}(C))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{(3),*}R_{S\times S\times S}((A\boxtimes B)\boxtimes C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{(3),*}(R_{S}(A)\boxtimes(R_{S}(B)\boxtimes R_{S}(C)))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{(3),*}(R_{S}(A)\boxtimes R_{S\times S}(B\boxtimes C))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{(3),*}R_{S\times S\times S}(A\boxtimes(B\boxtimes C))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{2}^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{i}}

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{1}^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{i}}

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

Here, the six unnamed natural isomorphisms are obtained by combining connection isomorphisms, external monoidality isomorphisms and
R
-transition isomorphisms along suitable paths; by
8.6
, we know that the resulting isomorphisms do not depend on the chosen paths. Since the central rectangle in the latter diagram is commutative by axiom (mor-
a
ETS), it now suffices to show that the remaining six lateral pieces are commutative as well. This can be achieved by the usual trick; we omit the details.
∎

In the same spirit, let us now study the notion of symmetry for internal and external tensor structures on morphisms.

.

Finally, let us study the notion of unitarity for internal and external tensor structures on morphisms.

Definition 8.20
.

Let
(\otimes_{1},m_{1})
and
(\otimes_{2},m_{2})
be internal tensor structures on
\mathbb{H}_{1}
and
\mathbb{H}_{2}
, respectively; let
\rho
be an internal tensor structure on
R
(with respect to
(\otimes_{1},m_{1})
and
(\otimes_{2},m_{2})

Let
u_{1}
and
u_{2}

\mathbf{1}^{(1)}
and
\mathbf{1}^{(2)}
on
(\otimes_{1},m_{1})
and
(\otimes_{2},m_{2})
, respectively, and let
w:R(\mathbf{1}^{(1)})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbf{1}^{(2)}
be an isomorphism between section of
\mathbb{H}_{2}
. We say that
\rho
is
unitary via
w
(with respect to
u_{1}
and
u_{2}
) if it satisfies the following additional condition:

(mor-
u
ITS)

For every
S\in\mathscr{S}
, the two diagrams of functors
\mathbb{H}_{1}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}_{2}(S)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(A)\otimes_{2}R_{S}(\mathbf{1}^{(1)}_{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(A)\otimes_{2}\mathbf{1}^{(2)}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(A\otimes_{1}\mathbf{1}^{(1)}_{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(A)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{2,r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{1,r}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(\mathbf{1}^{(1)}_{S})\otimes_{2}R_{S}(B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{(2)}_{S}\otimes_{2}R_{S}(B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(\mathbf{1}^{(1)}_{S}\otimes_{1}B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(B)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{2,l}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{1,l}}

are commutative.

Definition 8.21
.

Let
(\boxtimes_{1},m_{1})
and
(\boxtimes_{2},m_{2})
be external tensor structures on
\mathbb{H}_{1}
and
\mathbb{H}_{2}
, respectively; let

(with respect to

and

and

and

\mathbb{H}_{2}

is

and

(mor-
u
ETS)

S_{1},S_{2}\in\mathscr{S}
, the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}_{1}(S_{1})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}_{2}(S_{1}\times S_{2})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{1}}(A_{1})\boxtimes_{2}R_{S_{2}}(\mathbf{1}^{(1)}_{S_{2}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{1}}(A_{1})\boxtimes_{2}\mathbf{1}^{(2)}_{S_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{1}R_{S_{1}}(A_{1})}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}^{(2)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}^{(1)}}

and the diagram of functors

Lemma 8.22
.

j=1,2

(with respect to

(with respect to

, we have to show that the diagram of functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{1}}(A_{1})\boxtimes_{2}R_{S_{2}}(\mathbf{1}^{(1)}_{S_{2}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{1}}(A_{1})\boxtimes_{2}\mathbf{1}^{(2)}_{S_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{1}R_{S_{1}}(A_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}(A_{1}\boxtimes_{1}\mathbf{1}^{(1)}_{S_{2}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}(pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{e}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{1,r}^{e}}

and the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}_{1}(S_{2})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}_{2}(S_{1}\times S_{2})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{1}}(\mathbf{1}^{(1)}_{S_{1}})\boxtimes_{2}R_{S_{2}}(A_{2})}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}(pr^{12,*}_{2}A_{2})}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{1}R_{S_{1}}(A_{1})\otimes_{2}pr^{12,*}_{2}R_{S_{2}}(\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}}^{(1)})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{1}R_{S_{1}}(A_{1})\otimes_{2}pr^{12,*}_{2}\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}}^{(2)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{1}R_{S_{1}}(A_{1})\otimes_{2}\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}^{(2)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}(pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1})\otimes_{2}R_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}(pr^{12,*}_{2}\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}}^{(1)})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr^{12,*}_{1}R_{S_{1}}(A_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}(pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1}\otimes_{1}pr^{12,*}_{2}\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}}^{(1)})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}(pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1}\otimes\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}^{(1)})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}(pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{(2),*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}^{(2)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{(1),*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}^{(1)}}

that we decompose as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}(pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1})\otimes_{2}pr^{12,*}_{2}R_{S_{2}}(\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}}^{(1)})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}(pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1})\otimes_{2}pr^{12,*}_{2}\mathbf{1}_{S_{2}}^{(2)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}(pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1})\otimes_{2}R_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}(\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}^{(1)})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}(pr^{12,*}_{1}A_{1})\otimes_{2}\mathbf{1}_{S_{1}\times S_{2}}^{(2)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{(2),*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}^{(2)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{(1),*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{(2),*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}^{(2)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{(1),*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}^{(1)}}

Here, the lower central parallelogram is commutative by axiom (mor-
u
ITS) while the remaining pieces are commutative by naturality and by axiom (mor-
\mathscr{S}
-sect). This proves the claim.
∎

Lemma 8.23
.

For
j=1,2
let
(\boxtimes_{j},m_{j};u_{j})
be a unitary external tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}_{j}
with unit section
\mathbf{1}^{(j)}
, and let
(\otimes_{j},m_{j}^{i};u_{j}^{i})
denote the unitary internal tensor structure obtained from it via
2.11
and
5.4
.
Moreover, let
\rho
be an external tensor structure on
R
(with respect to
(\boxtimes_{1},m_{1})
and
(\boxtimes_{2},m_{2})
), and let
\rho^{i}
denote the corresponding internal tensor structure on
R
(with respect to
(\otimes_{1},m_{1}^{i})
and
(\otimes_{2},m_{2}^{i})
).

Suppose that
\rho
is unitary via
w
(with respect to
u_{1}
and
u_{2}
). Then
\rho^{i}
is unitary via
w
(with respect to
u_{1}^{i}
and
u_{2}^{i}
).

Proof.

Let us check that the internal tensor structure
\rho^{i}
satisfies condition (mor-
u
ITS): given
S\in\mathscr{S}
, we have to show that the two diagrams of functors
\mathbb{H}_{1}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}_{2}(S)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(A)\otimes_{2}R_{S}(\mathbf{1}^{(1)}_{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(A)\otimes_{2}\mathbf{1}^{(2)}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(A\otimes_{1}\mathbf{1}^{(1)}_{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(A)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{2,r}^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{1,r}^{i}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(\mathbf{1}^{(1)}_{S})\otimes_{2}R_{S}(B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}^{(2)}_{S}\otimes_{2}R_{S}(B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(\mathbf{1}^{(1)}_{S}\otimes_{1}B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(B)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{2,l}^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{1,l}^{i}}

are commutative. We only show the commutativity of the first diagram; the case of the second diagram is completely analogous. Unwinding the various definitions, we obtain the more explicit diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}(R_{S}(A)\boxtimes R_{S}(\mathbf{1}_{S}^{(1)}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}(R_{S}(A)\boxtimes_{2}\mathbf{1}_{S}^{(2)})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}R_{S\times S}(A\boxtimes_{1}\mathbf{1}_{S}^{(1)})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}pr^{12,*}_{1}R_{S}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}\Delta_{S}^{*}(A\boxtimes_{1}\mathbf{1}_{S}^{(1)})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(\Delta_{S}^{*}pr^{12,*}_{1}A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(A)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}

that we decompose as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{S}^{*}R_{S\times S}(pr^{12,*}_{1}A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{r}}

∎

Remark 8.24
.

The considerations collected in
8.8
admit obvious refinements to the subclasses of associative or symmetric or unitary tensor structures, both in the internal and in the external setting.

9.
Remarks on the triangulated case

In this final section, we consider the case of monoidal triangulated fibered categories in more detail. Our main goal is to explain how the various notions collected in the previous sections should be adapted in the case of triangulated fibered categories in order for them to interact well with the triangulated structure; in fact, the main point is to spell out their compatibility with the shifting functors.

As usual, we work over a fixed base category
\mathscr{S}
; when discussing (external) tensor structures on
\mathscr{S}
-fibered categories, we will ask
\mathscr{S}
to admit binary products.

9.1.
Triangulated fibered categories

In the first place, we complete the general preliminaries of
Section

1
by reviewing triangulated
\mathscr{S}
-fibered categories and morphisms of such.
For notational convenience, whenever considering a triangulated functor, we write the associated compatibility isomorphisms with shifting functors as mere equalities. In order to avoid confusion, until the end of the present subsection we indicate all connection isomorphisms of
\mathscr{S}
-fibered categories explicitly (rather than also as equalities as stated in
2.1
).

Definition 9.1
.

A
triangulated
\mathscr{S}
-fibered category

\mathbb{H}
is the datum of

•

for every
S\in\mathscr{S}
, a triangulated category
\mathbb{H}(S)
,

•

for every morphism
f:T\rightarrow S
in
\mathscr{S}
, a triangulated functor

f^{*}:\mathbb{H}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(T),

called the
inverse image functor
along
f
,

•

for every pair of composable morphisms
f:T\rightarrow S
and
g:S\rightarrow V
in
\mathscr{S}
, a natural isomorphism of functors
\mathbb{H}(V)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(T)

\mathsf{conn}=\mathsf{conn}_{f,g}:(gf)^{*}A\xrightarrow{\sim}f^{*}g^{*}A,

called the
connection isomorphism
at
(f,g)

satisfying conditions (
\mathscr{S}
-fib-
0
) and (
\mathscr{S}
-fib-
1
) of
1.1
plus the following additional condition:

(tri
\mathscr{S}
-fib)

For every pair of composable morphisms
f:T\rightarrow S
,
g:S\rightarrow V
in
\mathscr{S}
, the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(V)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(T)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(gf)^{*}(A[1])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((gf)^{*}A)[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}g^{*}(A[1])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}((g^{*}A)[1])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f^{*}g^{*}A)[1]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{conn}_{f,g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{conn}_{f,g}}

is commutative.

Definition 9.2
.

Let
\mathbb{H}_{1}
and
\mathbb{H}_{2}
be triangulated
\mathscr{S}
-fibered categories in the sense of
9.1
.

(1)

A

\mathscr{S}
-fibered categories

R:\mathbb{H}_{1}\rightarrow\mathbb{H}_{2}
is the datum of

•

for every
S\in\mathscr{S}
, a triangulated functor

R_{S}:\mathbb{H}_{1}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}_{2}(S),

•

for every morphism
f:T\rightarrow S
in
\mathscr{S}
, a natural isomorphism of functors
\mathbb{H}_{1}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}_{2}(T)

\theta=\theta_{f}:f^{*}R_{S}(A)\xrightarrow{\sim}R_{T}(f^{*}A),

called the
R
-transition isomorphism
along
f

satisfying condition (mor-
\mathscr{S}
-fib) of

(1) plus the following additional condition:

(mor-tri
\mathscr{S}

For every morphism

in
\mathscr{S}

\mathbb{H}_{1}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}_{2}(T)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}R_{S}(A[1])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}(R_{S}(A)[1])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f^{*}R_{S}(A))[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{T}(f^{*}(A[1]))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{T}((f^{*}A)[1])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(R_{T}(f^{*}A))[1]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{f}}

is commutative.

(2)

If we are already given a family of triangulated functors
\mathscr{R}:=\left\{R_{S}:\mathbb{H}_{1}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}_{2}(S)\right\}_{S\in\mathscr{S}}
, we say that an
\mathscr{S}
-structure on
\mathscr{R}
in the sense of
1.1
(2) is
triangulated
if the resulting morphism of
\mathscr{S}
-fibered categories

satisfies condition (mor-tri
\mathscr{S}
-fib).

9.2.
Triangulated tensor structures

From now on, we assume that
\mathscr{S}
admits binary products. Throughout this section, we consider one fixed
\mathscr{S}
-fibered category
\mathbb{H}
, which we assume to be triangulated in the sense of
9.1
.

Definition 9.3
.

(1)

A
triangulated internal tensor structure

(\otimes,m)
on
\mathbb{H}
is the datum of

•

for every
S\in\mathscr{S}
, a bi-triangulated functor

-\otimes-=-\otimes_{S}-:\mathbb{H}(S)\times\mathbb{H}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S),

called the

over

,

•

for every morphism
f:T\rightarrow S
in
\mathscr{S}
, a natural isomorphism of functors

m=m_{f}:f^{*}A\otimes f^{*}B\xrightarrow{\sim}f^{*}(A\otimes B),

called the

along
f

satisfying condition (

plus the following two additional conditions:

(triITS-1)

For every

, the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S)\times\mathbb{H}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A[1]\otimes B[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A\otimes B[1])[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A[1]\otimes B)[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A\otimes B)[2]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

is anti-commutative.

(triITS-2)

For every morphism
f:T\rightarrow S
in
\mathscr{S}
, the two diagrams of functors
\mathbb{H}(S)\times\mathbb{H}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(T)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}(A[1])\otimes f^{*}B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}(A[1]\otimes B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f^{*}A)[1]\otimes f^{*}B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}((A\otimes B)[1])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f^{*}A\otimes f^{*}B)[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}(A\otimes B)[1]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}A\otimes f^{*}(B[1])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}(A\otimes B[1])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}A\otimes(f^{*}B)[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}((A\otimes B)[1])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f^{*}A\otimes f^{*}B)[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}(A\otimes B)[1]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}

are commutative.

(2)

Let
(\otimes,m)
and
(\otimes^{\prime},m^{\prime})
be two triangulated internal tensor structures on
\mathbb{H}
. An equivalence of internal tensor structures
e:(\otimes,m)\xrightarrow{\sim}(\otimes^{\prime},m^{\prime})
is
triangulated
if it satisfies the following additional condition:

(eq-triITS)

For every
S\in\mathscr{S}
, the two diagrams of functors
\mathbb{H}(S)\times\mathbb{H}(S)\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A[1]\otimes B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A[1]\otimes^{\prime}B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A\otimes B)[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A\otimes^{\prime}B)[1]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes B[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes^{\prime}B[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A\otimes B)[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A\otimes^{\prime}B)[1]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}

are commutative.

Remark 9.4
.

The sign choice in axiom (triITS-1) above agrees with that of
[
Ayo07a
, Defn. 2.1.148]
. This choice is motivated by the behavior of the tensor product on the derived category of vector spaces over a field; the same rule holds for the bi-derived functor of any bi-exact functor between abelian categories.

Definition 9.5
.

(1)

A
triangulated external tensor structure

(\boxtimes,m)
on
\mathbb{H}
is the datum of

•

for every
S_{1},S_{2}\in\mathscr{S}
, a bi-triangulated functor

-\boxtimes-=-\boxtimes_{S}-:\mathbb{H}(S_{1})\times\mathbb{H}(S_{2})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S_{1}\times S_{2}),

called the
external tensor product functor
at
(S_{1},S_{2})
,

•

for every choice of morphisms
f_{i}:T_{i}\rightarrow S_{i}
in
\mathscr{S}
,
i=1,2
, a natural isomorphism of functors
\mathbb{H}(S_{1})\times\mathbb{H}(S_{2})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(T_{1}\times T_{2})

m=m_{f_{1},f_{2}}:f_{1}^{*}A_{1}\boxtimes f_{2}^{*}A_{2}\xrightarrow{\sim}(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}(A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2}),

called the

along
(f_{1},f_{2})

satisfying condition (
m

plus the following two additional conditions:

(triETS-1)

For every
S_{1},S_{2}\in\mathscr{S}
, the diagram of functors
\mathbb{H}(S_{2})\times\mathbb{H}(S_{2})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S_{1}\times S_{2})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}[1]\boxtimes A_{2}[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2}[1])[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A_{1}[1]\boxtimes A_{2})[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2})[2]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

is anti-commutative.

(triETS-2)

For every two morphisms
f_{i}:T_{i}\rightarrow S_{i}
in
\mathscr{S}
,
i=1,2
, the two diagrams of functors
\mathbb{H}(S_{1})\times\mathbb{H}(S_{2})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(T_{1}\times T_{2})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}^{*}(A_{1}[1])\boxtimes f_{2}^{*}A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}(A_{1}[1]\boxtimes A_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1}^{*}A_{1})[1]\boxtimes f_{2}^{*}A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}((A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2})[1])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1}^{*}A_{1}\boxtimes f_{2}^{*}A_{2})[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}(A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2})[1]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}^{*}A_{1}\boxtimes f_{2}^{*}(A_{2}[1])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}(A\boxtimes B[1])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}^{*}A_{1}\boxtimes(f_{2}^{*}A_{2})[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}((A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2})[1])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1}^{*}A_{1}\boxtimes f_{2}^{*}A_{2})[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{*}(A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2})[1]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}

\scriptstyle\sim

are commutative.

(2)

Let
(\boxtimes^{\prime},m^{\prime})
and
(\boxtimes,m)
be two triangulated external tensor structures on

. An equivalence of external tensor structures
e:(\boxtimes^{\prime},m^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\sim}(\boxtimes,m)
is
triangulated
if it satisfies the following additional condition:

(eq-triETS)

For every
S_{1},S_{2}\in\mathscr{S}
, the two diagrams of functors
\mathbb{H}(S_{1})\times\mathbb{H}(S_{2})\rightarrow\mathbb{H}(S_{1}\times S_{2})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}[1]\boxtimes^{\prime}A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}[1]\boxtimes A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A_{1}\boxtimes^{\prime}A_{2})[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2})[1]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}\boxtimes^{\prime}A_{2}[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2}[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A_{1}\boxtimes^{\prime}A_{2})[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A_{1}\boxtimes A_{2})[1]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}

are commutative.

We can then state the triangulated analogue of
2.12
as follows:

Proposition 9.6
.

The constructions of
2.10
and
2.11
canonically define mutually inverse bijections between triangulated equivalence classes of triangulated internal and external tensor structures on
\mathbb{H}
.

Proof.

In the first place, one needs to show that the constructions of
8.14
and
8.15
can be canonically promoted to the triangulated setting. Note that there exists an obvious way to make the single external and internal tensor product functors constructed in this way into bi-triangulated functors; it then suffices to check that this respects the additional conditions of
9.3
(1) and
9.5
(1). In the second place, one has to show that the canonical equivalences constructed in the proof of
2.12
are triangulated in the sense of
9.3
(2) and
9.5
(2). Both tasks are easy and left to the interested reader.
∎

9.3.
Triangulated associativity, commutativity and unit constraints

We go on by explaining how to adapt associativity, commutativity and unit constraints to the triangulated case, both in the internal and in the external setting.

Definition 9.7
.

Let
(\otimes,m)
be a triangulated internal tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}
.

(1)

A
triangulated associativity constraint

a
on
(\otimes,m)
is the datum of

•

for every
S\in\mathscr{S}
, a tri-triangulated natural transformation of tri-triangulated functors

a=a_{S}:(A\otimes B)\otimes C\xrightarrow{\sim}A\otimes(B\otimes C)

satisfying conditions (
a
ITS-1) and (
a
ITS-2) of
3.1
plus the following additional condition

(tri
a
ITS)

For every

, the three diagrams of functors

Proposition 9.9
.

The following statements hold:

(1)

The constructions of
3.3
and
3.4
define mutually inverse bijections between triangulated equivalence classes of triangulated associative internal and external tensor structures on
\mathbb{H}
.

(2)

The constructions of
4.3
and
4.4
define mutually inverse bijections between triangulated equivalence classes of triangulated symmetric internal and external tensor structures on
\mathbb{H}
.

(3)

The constructions of
5.3
and
5.4
define mutually inverse bijections between triangulated equivalence classes of triangulated unitary internal and external tensor structures on
\mathbb{H}
.

Proof.

One has to show that the two constructions mentioned in each statement can be canonically promoted to the triangulated setting. Note that, in each case, there exists an obvious way to make the single natural isomorphisms in the external and internal setting into multi-triangulated natural isomorphisms. Thus one only needs to check that this respects the relevant additional conditions listed in
9.7
and
9.8
. This is easy and left to the interested reader.
∎

Remark 9.10
.

Note that no additional condition is needed in the triangulated case for the various compatibility conditions involving associativity, commutativity and unit constraints discussed in
Section

6
. This should not be surprising, since these compatibility conditions are properties rather than additional structures.

9.4.
Triangulated tensor structures on morphisms

Finally, let us return to the setting of
Section

8
: we fix two
\mathscr{S}
-fibered categories
\mathbb{H}_{1}
and
\mathbb{H}_{2}
and a morphism of
\mathscr{S}
-fibered categories
R:\mathbb{H}_{1}\rightarrow\mathbb{H}_{2}
, which we assume to be triangulated in the sense of
9.2
.

Definition 9.11
.

Let
(\otimes_{1},m_{1})
and

and

on

is the datum of

•

plus the following additional condition:

, the two diagrams of functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(A[1])\otimes_{2}R_{S}(B)}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(A\otimes_{1}B)[1]}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{S}(A)\otimes_{2}R_{S}(B[1])}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(R_{S}(A)\otimes_{2}R_{S}(B))[1]}

Definition 9.12
.

Proposition 9.13
.

For
j=1,2
let
(\otimes_{j},m_{j})
be a triangulated internal tensor structure on
\mathbb{H}_{j}
, and let
(\boxtimes_{j},m_{j})
denote the triangulated external tensor structure corresponding to it (modulo triangulated equivalence) via the bijection of
9.6
.

Then the constructions of
8.9
and
8.10
canonically induce mutually inverse bijections between triangulated internal tensor structures on
R
(with respect to
(\otimes_{1},m_{1})
and
(\otimes_{2},m_{2})
) and triangulated external tensor structures on
R
(with respect to
(\boxtimes_{1},m_{1})
and
(\boxtimes_{2},m_{2})
).

Proof.

One has to show that the constructions of
8.9
and
8.10
can be canonically promoted to the triangulated setting. To this end, note that there exists an obvious way to make the natural isomorphisms in the external and internal setting into bi-triangulated natural isomorphisms. Thus one only needs to check that this respects the additional conditions of
9.11
and
9.12
. This is easy and left to the interested reader.
∎

Remark 9.14
.

Note that no additional condition is needed in the triangulated case for the associativity, symmetry and unitarity of a morphism as discussed in the second part of
Section

8
. Again, the reason is that these are properties rather than additional structures.
